#5282. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.

Washington, D.C., November 6, 2026 — The Digital Sovereignty Alliance (DSA), a nonprofit organization dedicated to advancing clear and ethical public policy, research, and education related to emerging technologies, participated in the Strobe Summit, where DSA Managing Director Adrian Wall moderated a fireside chat with former Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) Chair Christiopher  Giancarlo on the evolving regulatory landscape for digital assets and the future of financial markets.

Wall and Giancarlo also examined the regulatory challenges surrounding prediction markets, perpetual futures, and decentralized finance (DeFi). Topics included the CFTC’s role in overseeing event contracts, bringing offshore derivatives activity into regulated U.S. markets, the potential for 24/7 trading, and establishing accountability for decentralized platforms without traditional intermediaries. The discussion highlighted the need for regulatory frameworks that address market integrity, cybersecurity, customer asset protection, and the operational realities of increasingly interconnected financial markets.

DSA’s participation reflects its commitment to advancing informed policy dialogue and practical regulatory approaches that support responsible innovation, market integrity, and the integration of digital assets into the broader financial system.

The Digital Sovereignty Alliance (DSA) is a nonprofit social welfare organization committed to advocating for public policies that support ethical innovation in decentralized technologies, blockchain, cryptocurrency, Web3, and artificial intelligence. DSA conducts research, organizes educational events, and promotes policies that prioritize public welfare and digital sovereignty.

#5280. BlackRock Sells $105 Million of Bitcoin as Recovery Stalls Again

BlackRock, one of the world's leading asset management firms, has extended its prolonged Bitcoin selling spree, having continued to dump the asset since late October.

While BlackRock has yet to record any new Bitcoin purchase this month, the latest market data shows that it has sold Bitcoin again following the severe price downturn seen in the last day.

Latest data showcased on SosoValue shows that BlackRock's Bitcoin ETF has recorded another net outflow for the fourth time this week, as it continues to post negative daily trading sessions throughout the week.

Amid consistent withdrawals from investors, BlackRock has sold $104.97 million worth of Bitcoin after IBIT recorded another massive outflow on November 5th.

Although Bitcoin initially showed signs of a potential recovery earlier this week, the market has since reversed and the asset is back to trading in the red.

While the massive Bitcoin sale from BlackRock has arrived at a time when the market is facing another

volatility, it appears that institutional investors are rapidly withdrawing their funds and exercising caution to hedge against further losses.

Bitcoin has just witnessed its sharpest price reversal of the month, falling from a high of over $80,000 to a low of $75,114 within just 24 hours.

Following this sharp price drop, Bitcoin has seen a price decline of 5.22% over the past day, and it is now trading at $75,979 as of the time of writing.

Although the market has remained volatile for several weeks, the significant sell-off seen in the last day has sparked further concerns among both retail and institutional investors as expectations for a November price recovery begin to fade.

#5279. XRP bear trap alert! Bitfinex shorts surge 652% as supply tightens

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

The current market breakdown could go either way.

On one hand, this can lead to a further capitulation phase for the market as key support levels come under threat. However, it could also be seen as a bear trap for those short sellers who have stretched their positions too far. Notably, XRP seems to be at the forefront of this debate.

As the chart below shows, Bitfinex XRP shorts have spiked by 652.89% to 468.2 million. Given the weakening technical momentum, this bearish positioning is not entirely surprising. The altcoin has seen five straight  weekly lows and broken below the $1.20 support level, with its price now approaching the $1 mark.

Meanwhile, XRP’s relative strength is adding another layer of pressure.

After breaking out of its five-week consolidation, the XRP/ETH pair has dropped over 3% this week. This indicates that XRP is losing value to Ethereum, as traders are showing preference towards ETH in this current bear trend. This relative weakness in XRP is therefore contributing to the bearish sentiment.

From a technical standpoint, betting on more Ripple [XRP] downside, with $1 as the next target, looks like a calculated move rather than a random bet. However, on-chain signals are telling a different story, keeping the possibility of a bear trap on the table.

XRP is flashing textbook signs of a potential supply shock.

On the 5th of September, an estimated 13.24 million XRP was taken off exchanges, limiting the amount available to sell (as noted by one analyst). Moreover, whale holdings have increased by nearly 60 million XRP over the past week. While it is unclear whether these are the same tokens that left exchanges, both signals point to growing accumulation by large holders.

The key takeaway? XRP’s Binance Scarcity Index has hit its highest level since 2022. As shown in the chart below, the index has been deeply negative throughout September and October before making a sharp move higher in recent days. The index is now extremely positive, and it is one of its largest movements in almost 4 years. This indicates that XRP supply is becoming tighter on Binance.

Ultimately, XRP is showing a clear split between its technical and on-chain signals.

While negative momentum and increasing short interest indicate that there is more downside to come, market outflows, whale accumulation, and reduced Binance supply suggest that selling pressure may be losing steam. If these supply patterns remain, then those shorting the market hoping for a fall to $1 may be caught off guard, turning the current bearish setup into a potential bear trap.

Rising XRP shorts and weak price action point to a possible drop toward $1.

Whale buying and exchange outflows suggest supply is tightening, raising the risk of a bear trap.

#5278. Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Four flaws in Bitcoin’s consensus rules that have lingered for years.

Issues like quantum resistance may command attention, but a practical cleanup is what many experts think Bitcoin’s next soft fork is likely to address, spanning fixes for vulnerabilities in the network’s difficulty adjustment mechanism to denial-of-service vectors among miners.

Proposed in 2019 and known as the Great Consensus Cleanup (BIP-54), the upgrade seeks to address the “cruft” in Bitcoin’s consensus design through changes that have historically been sidelined, as described by long-time Bitcoin developer and BIP-54 co-author Matt Corallo.

“There’s a laundry list of things we know of that we really need to clean up,” he said during a recent appearance on MARA Foundation TV. “All of these issues are real concerns that could impact Bitcoin negatively, but they’re not like, ‘Oh, the sky is falling.’”

Although a formal timeline for the BIP’s undertaking hasn’t been established, Corallo thinks there’s an 80% chance that the Great Consensus Cleanup is up next for the network. During his talk with Head of MARA Foundation Isabel Foxen Duke, he also called it a practical exercise for Bitcoin developers who haven’t yet been through a soft fork.

At its core, Corallo said BIP-54 “fixes some stuff that everyone agrees are problems,” while targeting four specific vulnerabilities:

Still, not everyone views a maintenance-focused soft fork as a major win. On X, Paul Sztorc, co-founder and CEO of LayerTwo Labs, called BIP-54’s potential activation “kind of depressing, since it is a janitorial maintenance fork that doesn't do anything.”

Surveying developers at TABconf in Atlanta, Sztorc noted that Bitcoin’s “best and brightest” predominantly expect the Great Consensus Cleanup to be Bitcoin’s next soft fork anyway, with the earliest estimate in September 2027, and most pointing to 2028 or later.

Following the activation of Taproot in 2021, Bitcoin’s community has faced years of gridlock surrounding protocol changes. This friction is frequently framed around “ossification,” with broader protocol changes like OP_CAT (BIP-347) and OP_CHECKTEMPLATEVERIFY (BIP-119) struggling to reach ecosystem-wide consensus.

According to Corallo, soft forks have always involved overcoming technical hurdles. But more recently, he’s observed political and social friction that’s become just as relevant.

The Bitcoin community spent months debating how Taproot should be activated, with arguments pitting miner-signaling thresholds against mandatory user flag days. Corallo argues that BIP-54 can establish a deployment playbook for future soft forks.

“In every recent soft fork, there’s also been a bunch of drama about activation mechanisms,” he said. “So hopefully we could do a soft fork to go through that drama, and then copy and paste and reuse it and head off some of that drama [for future soft forks].”

In that sense, BIP-54 is just as much about proving Bitcoin can still agree on upgrades at the social layer as it is about actual code cleanup.

André Beganski is a Senior Content Manager at MARA Foundation, producing content on topics including the intersection of quantum computing and cryptography. He has previously worked for Decrypt.

#5277. Zcash (ZEC) OI Down 7.8% as NU7 Upgrade Goes Live

Zcash open interest has declined 7.8%, according to the data from analytical platform Coinglass.

Open interest (OI) refers to the value of futures contracts that are still open. In other words, the rise in OI means more active positions.

When a price and OI decline simultaneously, it usually suggests deleveraging. The traders might be closing positions or their positions might be liquidated.

ZEC is trading near $1,286, down 1.5% over the past 24 hours. The price’s pullback comes amid a broader market correction, with BTC down 5.75% in 24 hours.

Zcash gained 7.69% on November 4, rising on high expectations surrounding NU7. The upgrade introduces several changes to the network's transaction processing and reward structure.

As the upgrade went live on November 5, the target block interval went from 75 seconds to 25 seconds. According to the project’s statement, the change makes private payments confirm three times faster.

In addition to that, NU7 introduces a new transaction fee allocation model. 60% of the fees will go directly to a reserve for future network reserves, while the miners receive the remaining 40%.

Other changes include increased capacity for the Orchard shielded pool and new limits for wallet protection from spam.

So far, the market response has been mixed amid Friday’s correction. However, the ZEC chart is currently showing signs of an uptrend.

The price is testing support around $1,250, a level that could influence its next move. A sustained recovery above the $1,260-1,275 corridor would stabilize the further rebound.

On the other hand, a break below $1,230 would mean a failed recovery and a prolonged downtrend.

#5276. Same Price, Different Stellar: 69% Growth Outruns XLM On a Red-Day

Home » Altcoins » Same Price, Different Stellar: 69% Growth Outruns XLM On a Red-Day

Stellar continues to add regulated issuers, infrastructure partners, and measurable on-chain volume.

Stellar keeps stacking real-world asset activity and on-chain usage even as its token trades near multi-month lows.

On a day when the broader crypto market shed roughly $120 billion and XLM dropped about 9%, the network still ranked first among tracked chains in tokenized fund growth, adding $291 million.

The $291 million single-day print was not an isolated event.

Over the past ten days the network has added several institutional markers: Janus Henderson launched the first Nasdaq-licensed tokenized fund on Stellar, DTCC went live with Stellar as the first public chain in its setup.

Stellar DEX volume jumped 69% in a week, and almost all of it came from a single protocol.Aquarius, the liquidity protocol on @StellarOrg ($XLM), drove nearly the entire increase, per DeFiLlama.The data ⬇️The full story: https://t.co/ZuB0XcyCRC pic.twitter.com/AZKWxDxcrm

MetaMask began giving every account a Stellar address, Securitize signaled plans to expand, Bell went live on testnet, and Franklin Templeton’s presence on the network reached $1.1 billion.

These moves sit on top of longer-term figures. Stellar has repeatedly led or ranked near the top in tokenized fund inflows across 24-hour, 90-day, and year-to-date windows, even while XLM itself has lagged the network’s asset growth.

Separately, Stellar’s decentralized exchange volume rose 69% over the past week. Nearly the entire increase came from Aquarius, the network’s leading liquidity management protocol, according to DeFiLlama data.

Aquarius has been the dominant DEX by both volume and transactions for some time. Its 30-day volume recently sat near $95 million with cumulative volume above $800 million, and it has held the top spot by total value locked among Stellar DEXs.

🚨 BOOM: Crypto lost $120 billion yesterday. $XLM fell 9%.Same day, #Stellar ranked #1 of all tracked chains in tokenized fund growth: +$291 million.The last 10 days on Stellar:Janus Henderson launched the first Nasdaq-licensed tokenized fund.DTCC went live, with Stellar… pic.twitter.com/zgT0u6tW4C

The rest of the chain’s DeFi activity remains concentrated as well: a handful of protocols account for most of the locked value and trading.

The volume jump is real, yet total value locked on Aquarius has stayed relatively flat even during stronger trading months. That pattern suggests more activity is flowing through existing pools rather than a broad influx of new capital.

XLM is trading back near levels seen before some of the recent institutional announcements.

The gap between network metrics and token price has drawn direct questions from observers: who is required to buy more XLM as tokenized assets and DEX volume grow, and what mechanism turns that activity into sustained demand for the native token?

So far, Stellar’s progress in regulated funds and concentrated DeFi has not produced a matching lift in XLM.

Institutional products on the network often settle in other assets, and much of the on-chain trading runs through a small set of protocols whose fees and incentives do not automatically translate into broad XLM buying.

#5275. Cardano Node 11.2 brings Dijkstra closer, but why did its price fall by 6%?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Node 11.2 is expected to hit mainnet by 8th November, and it will bring Dijkstra features with it.

Usually, a big release would give the price something to work with. Not this time though.

Cardano Node 11.2 is out… only as a pre-release for now. The GitHub notes say it isn’t recommended for mainnet yet. The full release, likely tagged 11.2.1, is expected by the weekend (8th November).

The biggest change isn’t quite obvious though.

Node configs now come in a new “envelope” format; nodes older than 11.2 won’t be able to read them. Basically, this means operators will have to upgrade. The node’s API also got a boost. It can now run over a network address instead of only locally, and it picked up new tools for checking the mempool.

On the Dijkstra side, the CLI now handles sub-transactions. Dijkstra transactions can be submitted through the API too.

Leios is only groundwork here, and it’s switched off by default. The real thing is planned for Node 11.3, which is already being built.

… a $0.19 liquidity cluster is right above it.

The sell-off involved ADA falling from about $0.199 on 5th November to a low near $0.178 overnight.

The altcoin eventually clawed its way back to $0.186. However, at press time, it was still 6.7% below where it began.

The RSI on the short-term chart was at 48.3 too.

Additionally, the leverage has also taken multiple hits. Open Interest went down from about $197 million to $181 during the drop.

Funding seemed to be positive too with a reading of 0.0057%. This implied that longs haven’t given up yet.

Here’s what’s interesting, though. There appeared to be big pile of liquidations stacked on the heatmap, between $0.188 and $0.19. These seemed to be just above the press time trading price.

Prices cam often drift toward spots like that.

If Cardano pushes into that zone, those shorts could get squeezed… fast.

#5274. NYSE-backed crypto venture faces 90% stock rejection as $92 liquidity problem threatens tokenization dream

Apple, Nvidia and 65 other companies have objected to OKXICE's plans, while thin liquidity across existing tokenized stock markets raises a second major obstacle to its launch.

More than 90% of the stocks targeted by OKX and NYSE-backed tokenization venture now face objections from their issuers.

A comparison of OKXICE's latest regulatory notice with its original October proposal shows that companies have objected to 57 of the 63 stock tickers initially earmarked for trading.

The objections, which have grown to 67 companies overall, include Apple, Nvidia, Microsoft, Amazon, Tesla, and Meta, threatening to strip the proposed blockchain-based marketplace of many of Wall Street's most widely traded names before its launch.

The setback comes just weeks after the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) introduced a regulatory exemption designed to accelerate tokenized stock trading, allowing qualifying platforms to offer blockchain-based shares through automated trading pools.

OKXICE, a joint venture equally owned by crypto exchange OKX's US holding company and Intercontinental Exchange, the parent of the New York Stock Exchange, plans to let investors trade tokenized versions of US stocks around the clock using stablecoins.

However, the growing corporate resistance has left the venture facing questions about which securities it can offer and whether investors will have sufficient liquidity to trade them.

The scale of the resistance has grown rapidly since OKXICE outlined its plans in early October.

By Oct. 30, 50 companies had registered objections. That figure rose to 62 by Nov. 4 before another five companies, including Meta, Palantir, S&P Global, KLA and Super Micro Computer, joined the list on Nov. 5.

Of the original 62 companies identified by OKXICE, 56 have now objected. The original proposal covered 63 tickers because Alphabet's two share classes were listed separately.

That leaves just six companies from the initial lineup without disclosed objections: Strategy, Coinbase, Robinhood, Circle, Securitize and BitGo.

All six have substantial connections to the cryptocurrency or digital asset industry, highlighting how far the venture's potential stock selection has shifted from the broader US equity market.

However, the absence of an objection does not establish that those companies have endorsed the proposal or that their tokenized shares will be available when trading begins.

The resistance is particularly significant because of how the SEC structured its September exemption.

Under the framework, companies whose shares are tokenized by unaffiliated third parties must receive written notice and an opportunity to object. A formal objection prevents the venue from offering those third-party tokenized shares under the exemption.

The framework leaves room for issuer-backed tokenization, meaning the objections do not necessarily represent opposition to blockchain-based securities in general.

Nevertheless, OKXICE has yet to announce a launch date or confirm how many stocks will ultimately be available. Its public website continues to advertise the trading venue as forthcoming.

Even if OKXICE secures a viable selection of stocks, it faces another challenge: attracting enough capital to support trading.

The tokenization venture disclosed that neither it nor its affiliates will provide liquidity to its proposed marketplace.

Instead, eligible outside participants, including market makers and institutional investors, would need to deposit stock tokens and stablecoins into trading pools that facilitate transactions.

That model places considerable importance on the willingness of independent investors to commit capital, particularly outside traditional US market hours.

Existing tokenized equity markets offer a warning about the difficulties involved.

According to Dune's After Issuance report, the median decentralized trading pool for tokenized equities held just $92 as of Aug. 31.

Across 5,484 pools containing $69 million in assets, just 78 pools accounted for approximately $56 million, or 81% of the measured holdings. By protocol, Dune located $34 million on Uniswap, $17 million on PancakeSwap, and $11 million on Raydium.

This shows how concentrated liquidity remains across decentralized equity markets, despite growing interest in bringing traditional securities on-chain.

The $92 figure represents the median holdings of existing pools, rather than the liquidity available for every stock or a forecast of trading conditions on OKXICE.

The venture plans to use Uniswap v4-based pools on OKX's XLayer blockchain, where tokenized shares will trade against stablecoins through approved wallets.

Unlike conventional stock exchanges that match buyers and sellers through order books, the proposed system relies on assets deposited into pools to execute trades.

Insufficient capital in those pools could cause significant price slippage, particularly when investors try to execute large transactions or sell positions during market volatility.

That creates a significant challenge for a venture promising access to US equities beyond Wall Street's regular trading hours.

OKXICE must now overcome substantial issuer resistance while convincing outside market participants to finance trading in whatever tokenized stocks remain available.

Its eventual launch will provide the first meaningful test of whether the venue can assemble enough eligible securities and trading capital to deliver on its round-the-clock ambitions.

#5273. BSC Releases Reth Exploit Hotfix: Details About the Upgrade

BNB Smart Chain (BSC) has released a hotfix to its Reth execution client, BSC-Reth, according to the official announcement and a patch on Github. The patch addresses a vulnerability discovered on November 4, 2026, which could result in incorrect blockchain state-root calculations due to a bug in the legacy trie code (used to organize blockchain data).

Although the vulnerability could have resulted in financial loss, there is no evidence that the vulnerability was exploited or that funds were affected.

Rust Ethereum Harvester Tree, also known as Reth, is an execution client used to verify the data on the blockchain and process transactions. On November 4, 2026, a vulnerability was discovered in certain versions of Reth that could result in incorrect state-root calculations due to a bug in the legacy trie code.

This could potentially result in node disagreements and sync failures. The underlying bug was fixed in Reth pull request #28264, merged on November 3.

On November 6, BNB Chain published BSC-Reth v0.1.4-hotfix on GitHub. According to the release notes, this version includes a fix for incorrect state-root calculation in the legacy path in Reth. This version of BSC-Reth improves the way it deals with blockchain data, reducing the risk of incorrect state-root calculation during state verification.

All BSC-Reth node operators, including validators, MEV builders, full nodes, and sentry nodes, are advised to upgrade to this version and restart the node as soon as possible. This fix does not require a DB migration or hard fork. Users do not need to take any action. BNB Chain has also confirmed that this vulnerability has a limited effect on BSC-Reth and has no effect on opBNB.

#5272. Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas has sued Kalshi, accusing the prediction market of offering an illegal, unregulated gambling platform to the state's residents, Attorney General Tim Griffin said on Wednesday.

The lawsuit, filed against KalshiEX LLC and Kalshi Inc., alleges multiple violations of the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act. The state is seeking restitution, penalties, taxes and an injunction.

Today I filed a lawsuit against @Kalshi for violating the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act by operating an unregulated gambling platform.

Kalshi has built a global business that it calls a prediction market, allowing consumers to wager money on the outcome of just about… pic.twitter.com/ePycNKXzy6

— Attorney General Tim Griffin (@AGTimGriffin) November 4, 2026

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said. "And gambling on an unregulated platform is squarely prohibited by Arkansas law."

Instead of obtaining licenses and paying the taxes required of gambling operators, Kalshi opened its platform to Arkansas residents as young as 18 and encouraged them to bet on sporting and entertainment events, Griffin alleged. "Arkansas law is concerned with substance, not semantics," he said.

Griffin pointed to Kalshi's own responsible-gambling tools, including self-exclusion, deposit limits and trading breaks, along with its partnership with the National Council on Problem Gambling, as evidence that the company knows its platform carries gambling-style risks. "Although Kalshi insists that its products are not gambling, its actions speak louder than its labels," he said.

Kalshi, which is regulated by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, has argued in other cases that its event contracts fall under exclusive federal jurisdiction and are not subject to state gambling laws.

Arkansas is the latest of more than a dozen states to take on prediction markets. Among the most significant cases:

Other states have also moved against prediction markets. Arizona filed criminal charges against Kalshi in March, a Washington court temporarily blocked its sports contracts in July, and Massachusetts' case is now before the state's highest court after a lower court barred Kalshi's sports contracts in January.

The CFTC has sued at least 10 states to defend what it says is its exclusive authority over prediction markets, and President Donald Trump has backed the agency, calling several state officials who challenged the platforms "scum."

Federal appeals courts are split. The Third Circuit ruled in April that New Jersey cannot regulate Kalshi's sports contracts, while the Sixth and Ninth Circuits sided with Ohio, Tennessee and Nevada. New Jersey has asked the Supreme Court to settle the question, backed by 39 other states and the District of Columbia, as well as the NFL. Kalshi's response to the petition is due November 9, and the Supreme Court is not expected to decide whether to take up the case until December at the earliest, ESPN reported.

#5271. Solana's Skyline Crypto Ad Triggers Central Bank Investigation in China

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A joint operation of the People's Bank of China with Shanghai police clamped down on the Solana Accelerate China 2026 four-day tour. Authorities blocked the project's events in Shanghai, while the financial regulator in parallel launched an investigation against those locals who helped the blockchain platform with logistics and public relations.

What drew such a harsh reaction from the Chinese authorities was 'too aggressive' offline marketing of the tour. On the evening of October 14, a team associated with Solana, together with Shanghai contractors, put giant vibrant logos of the SOL token on the Citigroup Tower skyscraper facade in Lujiazui - the very heart of Shanghai's financial district. This was not just an ad: top managers, including Solana Foundation president Lily Lu, started publicly boasting about the opening of Shanghai's new office in a prestigious building.

All this massive campaign predictably caused an excitement in crypto circles. Even Binance founder Changpeng Zhao, known simply as CZ, expressed his amusement, calling 'amazing' the fact that Solana managed to not only do a promo in China, but get an office there as well. BNB Chain did not achieve such a success, added CZ.

It seems like the organizers tried to bypass the regulatory barriers using a positioning trick. The four-day tour between Shanghai, Hangzhou, Shenzhen, and Beijing was marketed strictly around technologies like AI agents, robots, IT development - not about crypto at all.

The maneuver did not get recognition in China to say the least, and the backlash did not take long to follow.

The morning session on October 16 in Shangri-La was shut down outright, with participants held for over an hour on the street after police suddenly arrived. Lily Lu tried to narrow the angles and stated that the cancellation was caused by registrations quadrupling, so the site was not ready to invite all registrants.

Fast forward to November 6 and representatives of the People's Bank of China, in an exclusive interview with the state agency Financial News, finally ended speculations on what actually happened. He directly called the skyline ad and all the aligned events an illegal marketing of virtual currencies. The Central Bank's position is 'black and white' there: SOL is a cryptocurrency and Solana's infra not only facilitates coins issuance, but also helps stablecoin turnover (especially USDT), which is banned.

Organizers in the face of Solana Foundation breached the crypto-risk agreement jointly issued back in February 2026 by eight Chinese authority departments. The document directly bans all the promotion of overseas crypto projects and implements individual liability for Chinese citizens and companies that help to do this.

Solana Foundation hasn't provided any official comments on the matter yet.

The signal by People's Bank of China is unambiguous though: full cleanup of all the mainland market out of foreign blockchain networks and activities associated with them.

#5270. Bitcoin wipes out $731M as election hopes fade: BTC’s next test is…

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Bitcoin [BTC] traders faced liquidations on both sides as prices surged and then erased the rally.

On the 4th of November, Bitcoin climbed from $75.7k to $81.2k, triggering $407.92 million in liquidations. Shorts accounted for $309.98 million of that total.

The reversal on the 5th of November brought another $323.54 million in liquidations, including $294.11 million in longs. Meanwhile, total crypto Market Capitalization fell $132.7 billion that day, bringing losses since the local peak to $149 billion.

Short-term bullish sentiment following the midterm election results was immediately overrun by an equities market pullback, with major AI stocks coming under selling pressure.

In a post on X, Glassnode observed that crypto traders are still here, despite the past month’s price retrace from $87k. Bitcoin [BTC] volume was at its lowest since 2023, before the August rally from $60k commenced.

Thereafter, volume remained above the August levels, which underlined sustained interest from traders and investors.

Rather than being a market collapse, the recent sell-off might be part of a healthy correction. Crypto intelligence platform Santiment posted that the crypto crowd became optimistic following the election results on the 4th of November.

This was a surprise, evidenced by the amount of short liquidations. Fueled by liquidations and FOMO, BTC briefly climbed past $81k.

Short-term FUD in reaction to selling in traditional markets across the globe sparked the Bitcoin reset. In both instances, the price went against the crowd’s expectations, showing why retail trader sentiment extremes can be counter-signals.

Crypto analyst Axel Adler Jr. wrote that the short liquidations reached the highest levels since late September. The Binance heist has also severely affected sentiment.

Since July, short-term holder BTC supply has expanded by 600k. It highlighted coins changing hands from long-term investors to newer market participants, and can also signal increased sensitivity to short-term price trends.

The True Market Mean was at $77.6k, and the brief foray above this level did not last. The metric measures the average cost basis of active investors while filtering out lost, dormant, or long-inactive coins.

A price slip below the TMM implied cooling market conditions and left investors at a loss on average. It could be a warning sign of a deeper correction.

#5269. Cheap online services routed web traffic through a White House-linked network, researchers say

Public services sell access to other people’s internet connections. Layer3 Intel says its tests repeatedly sent traffic through White House-linked addresses.

Threat-intelligence firm Layer3 Intel says public online services repeatedly sent its web traffic through a White House-linked network. The company recorded 236 observations between Oct. 5 and Nov. 3 across six internet addresses and 28 services, raising concern about outsiders using government-linked internet connections.

Layer3 identifies the network as hosting White House connectivity. AS2650 is listed under the name EOP-ASN in ARIN's registration record. The registration does not identify the device or person behind any particular connection.

Residential proxy services sell customers access to other devices' internet connections. As Layer3 describes the arrangement, traffic sent through a proxy appears to come from the device's network, rather than the customer buying access.

In its Nov. 4 report, Layer3 said it observed exits on 26 of the 30 days in its monitoring window. Its pipeline encountered the network while requesting global rotating access through residential proxy services, the company said, rather than deliberately selecting AS2650.

Layer3 said anyone could obtain the same type of access by paying a public residential proxy service a few dollars.

Layer3 said it cross-checked the addresses against public IP context pages from proxy-detection firms Spur and Synthient. The company said those pages also identified the addresses as residential proxy exits, but it has not disclosed the six addresses in its published finding.

Those figures describe recorded exits, public addresses and proxy services. They do not establish how many devices were involved or that each observation was an attack.

ARIN registers AS2650 to the Executive Office of the President. Layer3 reports 236 exit observations during Oct. 5–Nov. 3, 2026.

Services, public addresses and exit observations measure different parts of Layer3's finding.

The raw logs were not independently examined. Public-address counts are not device counts; internal access is not demonstrated.

Source-bound schematic using reported counts and independent ARIN registration. Dots represent 28 services and six public addresses; connectors show conceptual stages, not measured per-service mappings, traffic volumes or internal topology. No derived arithmetic or per-day chart reconstruction.

The raw logs were not independently examined. Public-address counts are not device counts; internal access is not demonstrated.

Layer3 said about half the activity occurred between 5 p.m. and midnight Eastern, with almost none in the early morning. It interpreted that pattern as suggesting a person's device running proxy software, rather than a public guest Wi-Fi network.

The company described an infected device with a proxy software development kit (SDK) as its explanation for the pattern, while acknowledging that its data shows neither the device nor the person behind the exits.

The distinction also matters for the security implications. Layer3 warned that poorly secured proxy software can, in some circumstances, expose internal systems. Its warning about this network was conditional: an SDK would need to have the kind of flaw documented in earlier proxy research for that route into internal systems to exist.

The published observations do not demonstrate access to internal White House systems. CryptoSlate has not reviewed the raw logs.

Layer3 said it had backed up its activity logs and was willing to share them with relevant agencies through its contact page.

RuntimeWire reported that founder Sergiy Usatyuk told it Layer3 would not disclose the specific addresses because of the sensitivity of the findings and had not notified anyone.

KrebsOnSecurity reported on Nov. 4 that the White House did not immediately respond to its request for comment.

#5268. SHIB Rekindles $1 Dream With Burns In Spotlight

Home » Shiba Inu (SHIB) » SHIB Rekindles $1 Dream With Burns In Spotlight

The vibrant community behind the popular memecoin just revived the $1 debate: are burns the main catalyst?

Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) official X account just quoted a rather optimistic outlook on the popular meme coin’s path forward, assessing the frequently-discussed $1 price target. Shiba Inu’s (SHIB) price tends to swing harder than the major-cap altcoins like Solana (SOL) or Ethereum (ETH) due to the speculative nature of the token.

A few days back, a crypto currency comparison between the major-caps surfaced online, and SHIB was included. In the insight, it was established that Shiba Inu (SHIB) “acts as a high-beta asset”. The analyst called it trading way below its potential, struggling due to the 589 trillion supply that makes a $1 tag “mathematically improbable, but NOT IMPOSSIBLE.”

The Shiba Inu team then shared the post with its followers and called it “nice work.” However, it’s of crucial importance to note that the Shiba Inu (SHIB) price portrayed in the graphic is overstated. The comparison table shows a $0.000012 price on the popular Japanese guard dog-themed coin. Realistically, these levels haven’t been recouped in 2026 yet.

No doubt, the big question is how to erase the 589 trillion SHIB tokens that are in circulation. At $1 each, the token’s market cap would reach about $589 trillion. That figure is simple arithmetic, price multiplied by supply, not the amount of money buyers would need to put in. Notably, this figure has considerably dropped from the initial 999 trillion due to community burns.

Presently, beyond 41% of the total supply has been torched. The regular SHIB burning by separate individuals and community initiatives has pushed SHIB’s weekly burn rate up by 190.45%, but it still revolves around millions of tokens rather than billions burned. Gradually, the stats could pick up along with the usage of Shibarium, the community’s native Layer-2 chain.

#5266. Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

A Canadian man has been extradited to the United States to face charges that he helped hack into university computers to steal login credentials and secretly mine cryptocurrency, the Justice Department said on Tuesday.

A federal grand jury in the Western District of New York indicted Ryan James Roach, 35, of Saskatchewan, and Mathiew James Stubbings, 41, of Ontario, on charges of conspiracy to obtain information from and damage a protected computer, obtaining information from a protected computer, and intentionally damaging a protected computer. The charges carry a maximum sentence of 10 years in prison and a $250,000 fine.

Canadian man extradited to the U.S. for his role in computer hacking scheme targeting universities... @FBIBuffalo @USDOJ_Intl https://t.co/vY8IacXdZZ pic.twitter.com/NsSZ5BIkIf

Between June 2016 and December 2019, the pair targeted U.S. educational institutions with supercomputers, researching security weaknesses in their networks before breaking in, according to the indictment. Once inside, they allegedly deployed malware, stole login credentials and forced the victims' computers to mine cryptocurrency for their own benefit.

Victims included institutions in New York, Tennessee, North Carolina and Massachusetts, among them a university in the Western District of New York, prosecutors said. The pair allegedly used virtual private servers in several countries, registered under false names, to send malware and mining software to victim computers and store stolen credentials, and routed attacks through computers compromised at other victims to hide their identities.

Roach was extradited from Canada on October 28 and arraigned in Rochester, New York, where he was held pending a detention hearing. Extradition proceedings against Stubbings are continuing in Canada.

The scheme is a form of cryptojacking, in which attackers use other people's computers to mine crypto while the victims bear the electricity costs and slower performance. The Justice Department did not say which cryptocurrency the pair allegedly mined.

But hackers looking to profit from stolen computing power may be turning elsewhere. In a report published Monday, Google's Threat Intelligence Group said it expects attackers to "increasingly prioritize LLMJacking over traditional resource theft like cryptojacking," hijacking companies' access to AI models and passing on the bills.

Unlike the gradual drain of cryptojacking, LLMjacking can run up huge cloud charges within hours, Google said. In incidents investigated by its Mandiant unit this year, fraudulent charges topped $100,000 in a single day, and some organizations racked up $1 million before access was cut off. Attackers are reselling stolen access through underground services that Google calls "LLM Mixers."

#5265. Is crypto’s 2026 outlook at risk as regulatory FUD returns to Washington?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

After the election, investors are looking for clues about crypto’s regulatory future.

However, one thing is clear: the road ahead remains uncertain, with volatility already creeping into the market. The impact of the recent election goes beyond the broader U.S. macro environment, extending into the regulatory landscape as well. The crypto industry has seen huge gains under Republican leadership in the U.S. with President Donald Trump’s vision of making the country the “crypto capital of the world.”

However, with Republicans holding a narrow majority in Congress, investors are becoming more cautious about what comes next. The recent challenges faced with the CLARITY Act, and the increased focus from the SEC around tokenizations, are adding to the uncertainty in the regulatory outlook.

That said, signs of a regulatory shift are already starting to emerge.

In the latest development, House Democrats are drafting a new bill regarding how crypto should be structured moving forward. The bill includes expanding the SEC’s jurisdiction and will also include provisions excluded from the CLARITY Act. This comes ahead of any potential change in who controls Congress.

From a regulatory standpoint, tighter SEC oversight could mean stricter compliance rules, increased scrutiny of crypto firms, and greater regulatory pressure on digital asset companies operating in the U.S. The key takeaway? Regulatory pressure may not stop at tighter SEC oversight.

Senator Elizabeth Warren, known to be a strong crypto critic, is expected to become the Chair of the Senate Banking Committee, potentially giving her greater influence over financial regulation and crypto policy.

Is regulatory FUD shaping up to be the next major bearish catalyst for the broader crypto market?

The recent political upheaval in the U.S. Congress has already started to affect regulation. Discussions around a new crypto market structure bill, increasing anti-crypto sentiment and potential increased scrutiny from the SEC are adding a new layer of uncertainty to an already volatile market.

Further adding to the concern, Bitcoin [BTC] is looking bleak on its yearly chart. Historically, Bitcoin has never closed consecutive years in the red. However, this could change soon. Bitcoin is currently trading around 12% lower on its 2026 yearly candle, with just two months left before the year closes. This follows a red candle in 2025, putting BTC at risk of recording its first-ever back-to-back years of annual losses.

In essence, Bitcoin’s next move may depend on more than just market sentiment.

As regulatory uncertainty increases amid potential increased attention from the SEC, Bitcoin has another factor to contend with. If selling pressure continues, BTC could be heading toward its first-ever back-to-back annual losses, breaking a decade-long historical trend.

A new crypto bill could give the SEC more control, raising concerns about stricter crypto regulations.

Bitcoin is down 12% in 2026 and risks its first-ever two consecutive years of annual losses.

#5264. Bank of England raises rates to 4% as it forecasts inflation near 5% next year

Energy-driven UK tightening raises a financing question for Bitcoin investors who borrow in sterling.

The Bank of England raised its benchmark interest rate by 25 basis points to 4% on Nov. 5 as a prolonged energy shock increased its inflation concerns. The move highlights a potential source of pressure on Bitcoin investors exposed to sterling borrowing. Bank of England decision

Eight policymakers backed the increase, while Catherine Mann preferred a larger, 50-basis-point move to 4.25%. The Bank projected UK consumer-price inflation around 5% in the second quarter of 2027, following September's 3.5% reading. The median in the Bank's Market Participants Survey already expected a 4% rate in November, which limits the case for treating the announcement as a fresh market shock.

The decision came despite limited evidence so far that the energy shock was spreading into broader wage and price-setting. Policymakers judged the pressure building in the economy sufficient to act, with stronger-than-expected activity adding to the case. That leaves persistence, including firms passing on costs and workers seeking higher pay, central to the next policy decisions. November minutes

UK borrowing costs were already rising. Forward market interest rates were feeding into rates faced by some households and firms, and benchmark two-year mortgage rates had risen nearly 50 basis points since the beginning of September. Business lending growth remained strong, showing that financing pressures were uneven. UK borrowing conditions

For investors exposed to sterling borrowing, higher financing costs could leave less room for Bitcoin positions. A broader reduction in willingness to take risk could also curb allocations.

Historical research supports the risk-taking mechanism. An IMF working paper published in August 2023 found that US Federal Reserve tightening reduced an aggregate crypto price factor through that channel. The factor's increasing correlation with equities coincided with institutional investors entering crypto. The finding concerns historical US policy and aggregate crypto prices, which limits its application to this UK decision. IMF working paper

#5263. Binance Issues Crucial Update for Hyperliquid (HYPE) and Litecoin (LTC)

Binance has issued another important update on the batch re-opening of withdrawal services for tokens previously listed on Binance after the recent $6 billion attack forced the exchange to suspend the feature.

While Binance has remained consistent with the reopening of withdrawals for crypto assets in batches since the attack, Hyperliquid has drawn different attention as it remains the only top 10 cryptocurrency by market capitalization that has not been re-enabled since the attack.

Earlier today, Binance announced a deadline for the resumption of withdrawals for Hyperliquid (HYPE), Litecoin (LTC), and four other cryptocurrencies.

The announcement has come amid growing criticism from Hyperliquid holders who have remained affected by the delay in Hyperliquid's withdrawals on Binance.

Although Binance revealed that it is working to restore withdrawals for HYPE, LTC, Injective (INJ), Aptos (APT), Plasma (PLASMA), and NEAR Protocol (NEAR) by the end of next week, the criticism has grown even louder, with holders tagging Binance's co-founder Changpeng Zhao as unfair.

Despite Binance's appeal, the announcement has only triggered further backlash from the Hyperliquid community as they continue to question the reason behind the delay, since Binance has progressively restored withdrawals for dozens of other cryptocurrencies in the past week.

The growing criticism from the Hyperliquid community against Binance has given rise to several allegations against the exchange, with holders questioning the genuineness of the attack.

Notably, Hyperliquid holders have called on Binance to give clear explanations for excluding HYPE from earlier withdrawal restorations, arguing that there was no reason to keep the token locked since Hyperliquid was not affected by the attack.

Given Hyperliquid's growing adoption in the decentralized derivatives market, some critics have alleged that Binance is making intentional efforts to halt the asset's explosive growth.

#5262. Binance Cuts Off SignalPlus API Keys Over 'Potential Security Issue' at Trading Platform

Binance Cuts Off SignalPlus API Keys Over 'Potential Security Issue' at Trading Platform

Binance has removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus, a third-party crypto trading platform, after SignalPlus reported a "potential security issue," the exchange said on Thursday.

"Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe," the exchange said, describing the move as an "immediate precautionary measure."

Following a potential security issue reported by third-party platform SignalPlus, we have proactively removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus as an immediate precautionary measure.

Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe.

If you used SignalPlus, please… pic.twitter.com/0HYYkTAW2p

API keys let third-party apps and trading tools connect to a user's exchange account and place trades on their behalf. Removing them cuts off that connection, meaning any automated strategies running on Binance through SignalPlus will stop until users set up new keys.

Binance urged SignalPlus users to log into their accounts directly to review their positions and open orders. It recommended that they wait until SignalPlus confirms the issue is fully resolved before reconnecting, and that they always enable IP whitelisting, which restricts an API key to approved internet addresses.

The exchange also called on all third-party platforms integrated with Binance to review their security controls, watch for unusual activity and contact it immediately if they spot any concerns.

SignalPlus, a Hong Kong-based platform that provides trading tools for crypto options and derivatives, connects to several exchanges, including Binance.

The incident comes a week after Binance was hit by the largest hack in crypto history, in which attackers drained about $6.4 billion from its hot wallets on October 29. The FBI has attributed the theft to North Korean hackers it calls TraderTraitor, a claim Pyongyang has denied.

Binance has said the attack began when a fake recruiter persuaded one of its engineers to download a malware-laced coding assignment, and that the attackers then used stolen credentials to reach an internal wallet signing service.

Binance said on Wednesday that it has replenished about 98% of the stolen assets from its own funds, and is still buying the rest, mostly less liquid tokens, through market and over-the-counter channels. It has also taken a new proof-of-reserves snapshot covering 44 tokens, up from 10, which users will be able to verify once its zero-knowledge proof is generated in about 50 to 60 hours.

The exchange is still restoring services. On Thursday, it began reopening withdrawals for more assets in phases, prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. Deposits remain open.

#5261. RealFi Nears Ethereum Launch, Weighs XRPL Expansion

Home » Trendsetter News » RealFi Nears Ethereum Launch, Weighs XRPL Expansion

RealFi is targeting a December 1 Ethereum mainnet launch while exploring a possible XRP Ledger expansion.

RealFi plans to launch on Ethereum mainnet on December 1, according to a Cardano-focused host reviewing the project’s expansion plans. The Angry Crypto Show puts the move at the center of a broader question: whether Cardano-originated financial products can attract users and liquidity beyond their home network.

The host cites Token Terminal data showing RealFi has surpassed $121.7 million in asset transfer volume on Cardano. That figure measures transfers, not deposits or total value locked. The transcript does not specify the year of the planned Ethereum launch.

In remarks quoted from an interview with David Gokhshtein, RealFi CEO John O’Connor argues that stablecoins keep growing because they serve a practical purpose: moving stable-value money across borders. He identifies yield-bearing products as the fastest-growing part of that business.

An XRP Ledger expansion is less definite. O’Connor points to the network’s high fully diluted valuation and relatively limited DeFi activity as reasons to explore an integration.

“That’s a conversation we’re kicking off to see whether there’s value for us to come over there,” he says, according to the transcript. No launch date, partnership agreement or technical implementation is announced.

The host sees a potential exchange: Cardano-originated products could gain access to XRP users and liquidity, while offering additional financial functionality. That is the commentator’s investment thesis, not evidence of committed demand.

The video also reports that more than $450,000 worth of bitcoin had been supplied to Cardano’s Strike Finance within eight days of its integration of a bitcoin collateral asset. The figure offers another adoption signal, though the transcript provides no longer-term comparison.

Whether RealFi delivers its announced Ethereum launch, secures integrations and attracts sustained usage. An XRP Ledger expansion could broaden distribution, but O’Connor’s qualification matters – the project wants wider access only where the numbers justify it.

#5260. Why aren’t XLM crypto traders betting big before the 23 November vote?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Stellar’s [XLM] next upgrade just cleared its testnet vote. Validators will decide on the mainnet version in a couple of weeks.

Hence, the question – Has the token’s price moved in lockstep?

Bell (also known as Protocol 30), cleared its testnet vote on 3rd November. Validators will vote on the mainnet version on the 23rd.

According to the Stellar Development Foundation, Bell would bring three changes. The first will involve letting smart contract tell payments for different customers apart. This would make life immensely easier for businesses.

The second change will allow smart contracts check post-quantum signatures as the network’s own signatures would stay the same. The last would switch ledger timing to milliseconds. Here, it’s worth noting that they’ve admitted that this change along won’t make blocks faster.

Also, 23rd is not the final launch date. In fact, Bell only goes live if validators approve it, with the Foundation clarifying that the date could still move.

In the meantime, wallets, exchanges and node operators need to update their software.

Ideally, an upgrade would put the price up on its shoulders. Not this one though.

XLM went up to about $0.171 on the 4th, then came tumbling down the next afternoon. It’s been stuck between $0.153 and $0.157 since. At press time, it was valued at around $0.155.

Indicators seemed to be indecisive. The RSI was at 45.2, under the Signal Line. The ADX on the DMI was at around 18.5. What did this indicate? Well, there didn’t seem to be a strong trend either way, though sellers were just slightly ahead.

Futures traders have mostly taken a few steps back too. Open interest dropped from about $94 million to $88 million during the slide and at the time of writing, it was $89.5 million. Funding was barely positive at 0.0011% too.

Simply put, it may be evident that everyone’s playing it safe. No one’s betting big before the big 23rd November vote. For now, $0.16 is the level to watch.

#5258. Bitcoin ETFs bought nearly twice what miners produced in Q3—but $430 million left on Thursday

ETF buyers absorbed more than new issuance in Q3, but concentrated purchases and older-holder distribution complicate Bitcoin’s scarcity case.

US spot Bitcoin ETF outflows totaled $430 million on Nov. 5, according to Farside Investors.

Bitcoin's ETF buyers added 79,800 BTC in the third quarter, against 41,300 BTC newly mined, according to 21Shares' Nov. 2 report. The comparison supports the scarcity argument, but offers limited reassurance about demand today: most of that buying arrived in two short windows, while older-holder balances fell by much more.

For Bitcoin holders assessing a supply squeeze, the unresolved question is whether fund demand persists between those buying bursts. New issuance is only one part of the picture. Coins already held by existing investors can also move, so purchases exceeding mining output do not establish how much supply buyers must absorb at a given price.

The 21Shares figures cover Q3 through Sept. 30. Aug. 17-27 and Sept. 21-25 accounted for 91% of ETF inflows measured in Bitcoin.

The same report recorded a 248,000 BTC reduction in long-term-holder balances during Q3. That larger change puts existing holdings alongside issuance in the scarcity discussion. However, it is a net balance decline, not a measured total of executed market sales.

Q3 2026 through Sept. 30: fund accumulation outpaced issuance, while buying clustered into two windows. Report published Nov. 2.

A quarter's ETF purchases exceeding issuance do not establish persistent absorption of existing holdings.

ETF ownership and age-based cohorts may overlap. Holder balance decline is not executed sales. These categories cannot be added into a market shortfall. All displayed figures describe historical Q3 2026.

Reported BTC values shown on a common zero-to-250,000 magnitude scale; bars use absolute lengths and retain signed labels. Width = absolute BTC / 250,000 × plot width. Buying-window positions use day offsets within the 92-day quarter, inclusive endpoints. Date-band lengths do not encode flow volume. Arithmetic checked with a calculation tool.

ETF ownership and age-based cohorts may overlap. Holder balance decline is not executed sales. These categories cannot be added into a market shortfall. All displayed figures describe historical Q3 2026.

ETF ownership and age-based holder classifications also are not established as mutually exclusive. Subtracting the balance decline from ETF purchases, or adding it to issuance as a separate block of selling, would imply an accounting precision the figures do not provide.

Glassnode's long-term-holder position metric offers a more recent directional check. Its public page showed approximately negative 151,885 BTC in a 10:39 UTC capture on Nov. 6, labeled “24 hours ago.” That relative label does not establish a particular trading session.

The metric measures the rolling 30-day change in the cohort's supply. Coins mature into the long-term group as they age, while spending moves supply out. A negative reading means spending outweighs maturation, rather than that the displayed amount was sold during one day.

Glassnode classifies holders using an entity's averaged purchasing date, with gradual weighting centered on 155 days, and excludes exchange-wallet supply. The reading therefore describes a particular cohort, rather than all potential sellers. Its rolling window also differs from Q3, preventing a direct claim that distribution has accelerated or slowed.

The more persuasive recovery signal would be repeated ETF inflows alongside slower net distribution from older holders. Persistent redemptions with continued distribution would weaken the case.

#5257. Bitcoin ETFs could surpass mutual funds in AUM by 2028: Bloomberg analyst

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

As we are about to enter the third year of the Spot Bitcoin [BTC] ETF being launched, it appears that BTC ETFs have a significantly stronger influence on BTC price returns than corporate Bitcoin accumulation.

According to the latest analysis by Ecoinometrics, an inflow of 10,000 BTC into spot Bitcoin ETFs corresponds to an estimated 3.2% boost in monthly Bitcoin returns. In comparison, the same amount of Bitcoin purchased by public companies contributes approximately 0.7% to monthly returns.

This suggests that ETF purchases have roughly 4.5 times the impact of corporate buying on Bitcoin’s price performance.

Adding more to this analysis, Bloomberg’s James Seyffart suggests that ETFs could surpass mutual funds in total Assets Under Management (AUM) within the next two to three years.

Their growth is only accelerating. If we stay on the same trend we’ve been on since the summer it will happen in ~two years.

Confirming his prediction, Syeffart used a chart and highlighted that ETFs accounted for 40.21% of total fund assets as of September 2026. And given the recent growth trends, ETFs could reach the 50% mark as early as September 2028 if the three-month growth rate continues.

However, other projections estimate this milestone between November 2028 and December 2029, depending on the period considered.

At the same time, ETFs rolling 12-month growth rate have also reached 3.74% in September 2026, above the historical average of 2.78% since the 2020 COVID market bottom. This indicates that ETF adoption is expanding faster than its long-term average.

This comes as BTC ETFs are in their outflows streak. According to Farisde Investors data, from 1st to 5th November 2026, Bitcoin ETFs saw mostly outflows. In fact, so far in November, it has already recorded $869.82 million in outflows. The only exception being on the 4th of November, when the BTC ETFs recorded a net inflow of $74.6 million.

All of this happened as the price of BTC was trading at $76,102.46 after a drop of 52% in the past 24 hours.

That said, besides Bitcoin ETFs, Ethereum [ETH] ETFs also recorded $272.09  in outflows, and Binance [BNB] ETFs recorded $271.01K in outflows. Meanwhile, XRP ETFs were funded by $529.51K in outflows, Solana [SOL] ETFs had $1.18M in outflows, and Hyperliquid [HYPE] ETFs also saw $7.66 million in outflows.

In short, the pattern suggests risk-off sentiment across crypto at the start of November, and not just in Bitcoin. So, given the current data, it remains to be seen whether Seyffart’s predictions turn into reality or not.

#5256. Solana Targets Hong Kong's Tokenization Market

Solana Company seeks to broaden its footprint in Hong Kong amid rising interest from major financial institutions in tokenized assets in the Asian financial hub.

As reported by the South China Morning Post on Nov. 6, the Nasdaq-listed firm is seeking to leverage rising demand for blockchain-based financial products as the Asia-Pacific region emerges as one of the major growth opportunities for its business.

Solana Company Chairman and CEO Joseph Chee thinks Asia-Pacific could be one of the most exciting markets for Solana's ecosystem.

The firm already has its Asia-Pacific headquarters in Hong Kong and is positioning itself as a provider of digital asset infrastructure and related services.

Last month, Solana Company was listed as one of Hong Kong's strategic enterprises by the Office for Attracting Strategic Enterprises. The designation emphasized the firm's potential role in Hong Kong's burgeoning digital asset sector.

The firm now aims to identify opportunities to offer traditional financial institutions blockchain infrastructure, especially for their work in tokenizing real-world assets.

Hong Kong has been pushing for tokenization as part of its efforts to become a global digital asset hub.

During Hong Kong FinTech Week earlier this week, Securities and Futures Commission CEO Julia Leung announced that regulators had already approved 17 tokenized investment products for retail investors.

In total, the products manage around $1.3 billion in assets (a 34% surge from last year).

Although the tokenization industry has been growing, Chee admitted that Hong Kong is not as advanced as the U.S. when it comes to tokenization.

Even so, the fact that Hong Kong's tokenization industry is still nascent might be an opportunity for firms that could offer the necessary infrastructure.

Solana Company is now looking into boosting its local presence in Hong Kong to leverage the rising demand.

Chee said the firm might be ready to scale up its local workforce from fewer than 10 employees to as many as 30.

The next six months will be crucial for assessing the growth of the local market.

#5255. Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Samsung is putting digital dollars in the same app where its users keep their credit cards and boarding passes.

Stablecoin accounts went live in Samsung Wallet on Thursday for eligible U.S. Galaxy users, letting them buy, hold, send, and receive Circle’s USDC on the Solana blockchain without downloading a separate crypto app or managing private keys. The feature reaches about 82 million compatible Galaxy devices in the U.S., according to the announcement.

“For a decade, stablecoins have proved the technology works,” said Lily Liu, president of the Solana Foundation. “What they lacked was a way to reach the mainstream.”

Samsung first announced the feature on Oct. 7, when the electronics giant tapped Solana for USDC transfers. Samsung Wallet first teased plans to support the stablecoin, which is pegged to the U.S. dollar, over the summer.

The product is built with Bastion, a licensed stablecoin custodian and infrastructure provider, and Coinbase, which safeguards the USDC as Bastion’s sub-custodian through its Coinbase Prime Vault. Samsung’s disclosures stress that the account is a crypto wallet, not a bank account.

Users can send USDC to other Samsung Wallet users in the U.S. and to compatible outside crypto wallets with no Samsung transfer fee, though other providers may charge one. They can also send money to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, where recipients may receive local currency, with fees that vary by destination.

Topping up with a credit card, including through Samsung Pay, carries a 4% fee, and withdrawals to a bank account or debit card also come with a fee. New users who make a first top-up of $50 or more can receive $25 in USDC. The service requires Android 13 or later and is open to U.S. residents 18 and older, except those in New York.

"Stablecoins have the potential to make moving money around the world faster and easier, and we want Galaxy users to be able to take advantage of that without having to navigate the complexity of traditional crypto tools,” said Woncheol Chai, who leads the digital wallet team in Samsung’s mobile business. “Solana helps us bring that experience to Samsung's scale."

Samsung said it expects to explore online and in-store stablecoin payments and to expand to additional markets, subject to local regulation.

#5254. Binance cuts off trading platform SignalPlus after hackers break in with a fake Teams invite

Binance and Deribit disabled the platform’s connections to customer accounts. SignalPlus says the intrusion began with a fake invitation sent to an engineer.

Binance cut its connections with crypto trading platform SignalPlus on Nov. 5 after a security warning. SignalPlus later said attackers had broken into its network after an engineer was tricked with a fake Microsoft Teams meeting invitation.

API keys let software access an exchange account within the permissions a user grants. In its advisory, Binance described the revocation as an immediate precaution and said its own systems were unaffected and user funds remained safe.

Deribit later issued its own notice. Deribit said it had revoked all customer API keys connected to SignalPlus, stopping trading, monitoring and automation through that connection. It said Deribit's systems and data were unaffected, and directed users to check their accounts on Deribit itself.

SignalPlus subsequently described an attack it had detected in the early hours of Nov. 4. In a statement, SignalPlus put losses below $82,000 in its assessment to date and said it had begun reimbursing affected users.

Binance told SignalPlus users to check both positions and open orders through its own platform. Binance recommended waiting until SignalPlus confirms full resolution before reconnecting API keys, and enabling IP whitelisting.

Deribit's guidance covered open orders, positions and recent account activity. @DeribitOfficial also recommended IP allowlisting and waiting for official communication from SignalPlus before reconnecting. Its notice described the interruption to functions through SignalPlus, while directing users to manage their accounts through the exchange.

Binance and Deribit say they revoked SignalPlus-linked API keys and directed users to review their accounts on the exchanges themselves.

Check positions and open orders directly on the exchange, and wait for SignalPlus communication before reconnecting.

The advice extended beyond those two exchanges. In a post responding to Binance's advisory, Changpeng Zhao urged people using SignalPlus on other exchange platforms to revoke their API keys and advised against storing keys with third-party platforms.

Binance also called on third-party platforms integrated with it to review their security controls, monitor unusual activity and contact the exchange immediately if they detected concerns.

SignalPlus said its investigation traced the incident to a compromised Telegram account belonging to a contact at a third-party technology vendor. According to the company, the attacker impersonated that contact and sent an engineer a fake Microsoft Teams meeting invitation that compromised the engineer's computer.

SignalPlus said it detected the attack in the early hours of Nov. 4 and contained the intrusion. SignalPlus said it contacted partner exchanges to disable every user API key associated with its platform and suspended services for a comprehensive security review.

The company said full reimbursement had begun on a case-by-case basis and pledged to repay all affected users.

Binance's Spot API documentation separates permission to place and cancel orders from permission to view private account information, such as order status and trading history. Trading permission must be enabled explicitly. Binance gives the example of one key for trading and another for monitoring order status.

IP allowlisting adds a separate restriction. Binance Academy's guidance explains that, when IP restrictions are enabled, requests from addresses outside the key's whitelist are rejected.

SignalPlus acknowledged that it had not previously made IP whitelisting mandatory for exchange API access. It said it would tighten API access requirements and strengthen endpoint security, internal access controls and key management as part of its response.

SignalPlus said services would return in phases after its review, necessary patches and confirmation that restoration was safe. Its statement supplied no fixed reopening date.

#5253. Gnosis Pay Breach Exposes User Data, Triggering Immediate Phishing Alerts

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Gnosis Pay, a fintech project from Britain, officially confirmed that data belonging to holders of its retail Visa debit cards was compromised. The breach which took place on November 3 continues the trend of this fall, when attackers no longer need to go after blockchains or smart contracts. It’s far easier for them to hit the traditional fiat infra that links digital assets to the real world.

Institutional partners and investors can exhale as the company’s enterprise and white-label B2B platform is running normally. For ordinary users, the key point is that the crypto in their wallets is safe, as the company said funds were not affected either. Card issuer Monavate Limited, which is regulated by the UK’s Financial Conduct Authority, continues to operate as well. The hackers walked away with data only.

This time the unauthorized access involved a legacy B2C card processing system. The Gnosis Pay platform was already being prepared for retirement, with a full shutdown scheduled for December 20. According to the official explanation, after the hack happened, Gnosis Pay’s security team moved fast to contain the threat and cut off access via the compromised keys within two hours.

The Gnosis Pay incident rounds out a trilogy of major leaks this fall and exposes a weak spot shared by crypto-fiat gateways. The timeline of the past few weeks points to a clear pattern evident in Ledger, Trezor, SafePal and Keystone leaks throughout 2026.

Gnosis Pay has finished emailing affected users with a detailed list of the exposed data. Until the system goes offline next month, the company is operating in emergency mode with transaction monitoring being upped, and 3D Secure verification is now mandatory for all online payments.

Of course, the company has also issued an urgent phishing alert. Armed with purchase details, addresses and personal data, scammers are already starting to pose as official customer support. The Gnosis Pay team urges users to be suspicious of all incoming requests and reminds them that “we and our Partner Apps will never ask” for security credentials such as seed phrases, private keys, PINs or full card numbers to verify or cancel transactions. Finally, any account changes are legitimate only when users make them directly within the official app.
