#5199. Hyperliquid Strategies files to become CFTC-registered FCM, posts $13.3 million in quaterly revenue

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The filing would let the HYPE treasury firm carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 perpetual futures markets, pending regulatory approval.

Nasdaq-listed Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) reported $13.3 million in revenue for the quarter ended Sept. 30, exceeding analyst estimates of $10.6 million to $12 million, depending on the data provider.

The company posted $620.6 million in net income, largely driven by $803 million in unrealized gains on its Hyperliquid (HYPE) token holdings, partially offset by a $172.3 million income tax provision.

Perhaps most notably, Hyperliquid Strategies filed with the CFTC to become a futures commission merchant (FCM), which would allow the firm to carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 markets.

"Hyperliquid recently announced its intention to add additional deployer functionalities under the name HIP-3," the firm said in a release. "These optional additional features would allow deployers to build and launch permissioned markets on Hyperliquid's onchain infrastructure. Independent operators would run their own market in a compliant manner, while the chain underneath would stay a neutral infrastructure layer."

The FCM, which is subject to regulatory approval, would allow Hyperliquid Strategies to "play a role" in bringing perpetual futures to U.S. customers within a regulated framework.

"There can be no assurance as to the timing or outcome of the application or regulatory approval of permissioned HIP-3 markets," the firm said.

This comes as Hyperliquid's underlying infrastructure moves closer to the U.S. market. NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange agreed last week to partner with TradeXYZ to bring Hyperliquid-based perp futures markets to U.S. traders. ICE would operate the exchange and clearing infrastructure, with TradeXYZ designing the markets.

Earlier this year, President Donald Trump said his administration was working to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S. "in a fully compliant and legal fashion."

#5198. S&P 500 Plunges, Wall Street Fears Circuit Breaker, Just One Day After Trump Celebrated Record Highs

S&P 500 Plunges, Wall Street Fears Circuit Breaker, Just One Day After Trump Celebrated Record Highs

President Donald Trump's celebration of Wall Street's highs aged rather poorly. Less than 24 hours after boasting about Americans' retirement accounts going "through the roof," the S&P 500 plunged as much as 3.8% Thursday, bringing emergency trading halts into the conversation.

The selloff prompted traders to monitor the market's circuit-breaker thresholds, which can temporarily shut down trading across U.S. stock exchanges when losses become too severe. The S&P 500 would need to fall another 3.2 percentage points from its decline to trigger the first emergency halt.

The timing is particularly awkward for Trump, who has repeatedly pointed to stock market performance as evidence of his administration's economic success.

"Stock Market just hit another Record High—91 Days in just a short period of time—Less than two years. Your 401(k)s are through the roof," Trump wrote Wednesday on his Truth Social account. He closed the message with "Record Setting All! President DONALD J. TRUMP."

Wall Street had rallied Wednesday following the Democratic Party's sweeping midterm election victories. The S&P 500 gained 1.6%, its strongest daily performance in three months, while the Dow Jones Industrial Average jumped more than 1,500 points to 53.361 points.

Thursday's losses erase that rally in a single session, with technology stocks taking the biggest beating.

The U.S. stock market has three emergency thresholds designed to slow panic selling. These mechanisms, known as circuit breakers, automatically suspend trading when the S&P 500 falls by a specified percentage from its previous closing price.

The first threshold is a 7% decline, corresponding to an S&P 500 level of 7,150.41 points for Thursday's session. Reaching that level before 3:25 p.m. ET would trigger a 15-minute halt across US equity markets.

The circuit breaker level is located in the following chart’s blue line

A 13% decline, or 6,689.09 points, would trigger another 15-minute suspension. The most severe threshold comes at 20%, or 6,150.89 points, which would shut down trading for the remainder of the day.

Under the New York Stock Exchange's rules, the first two circuit breakers stop applying at 3:25 p.m. ET, while the 20% threshold remains active throughout the session.

The rules apply to the entire U.S. equity market, not just stocks included in the S&P 500. A market-wide halt would also suspend trading in companies listed on the Nasdaq and other US exchanges.

The technology sector was particularly hard hit as investors continued digesting the consequences of Tuesday's election results. At around 11 a.m. ET, the Nasdaq Composite had fallen nearly 5%, while the S&P 500 was down 3.2%.

Nvidia lost more than 6%, while the broader semiconductor index plunged nearly 11%. Companies involved in building AI infrastructure were also under pressure as investors worried that Democratic victories could bring stricter oversight of data-center construction.

"We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout," Ipek Ozkardeskaya, senior analyst at Swissquote Bank, told Reuters.

The selloff also spilled into cryptocurrencies. Bitcoin fell below $76,000, wiping out its post-election recovery as approximately $428 million in leveraged crypto long positions were liquidated within an hour, as Decrypt reported earlier today.

Stocks also faced pressure from persistent inflation concerns and uncertainty over the Federal Reserve's next interest-rate decision. Investors are awaiting Friday's October employment report, which could provide further clues about the direction of monetary policy.

Despite Thursday's sharp losses, the S&P 500 had not reached its first market-wide circuit breaker at the time of this report.

Cathie Wood's Ark Invest bought $2.44 million worth of Coinbase, while selling $2.27 million in Bullish and $2.28 million in Bitmine shares.

Cathie Wood-led investment firm Ark Invest bought additional shares of Coinbase on Thursday but trimmed its holdings in Bullish and Bitmine amid a sharp sell-off in U.S. technology stocks.

#5194. Bitcoin Price Holds Near $75,000 After Hot October Jobs Report

U.S. employers added 165,000 jobs in October, nearly three times the 59,000 economists expected, the Labor Department said Friday, and the bitcoin price briefly pushed above $76,100 before settling near $75,500, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows.

The unemployment rate edged down to 4.1% from 4.2%, beating the Dow Jones forecast that called for it to hold. Average hourly earnings rose 0.4% on the month and 3.1% over the year, both ahead of estimates, CNBC reported.

Prior months were revised higher as well. August payrolls moved to 186,000, an upward revision of 53,000, and September rose to 60,000 after a 31,000 upgrade, leaving the two-month total 84,000 stronger than first reported.

The print lands on a market still digesting Thursday’s Nasdaq-led rout. The bitcoin price had held near $76,000 into that close, dipped toward $75,200 late in the session, then recovered into the mid-$75,000s overnight before the 8:30 a.m. ET release. Spot bitcoin changed hands around $75,800 after the data, with a market cap of about $1.52 trillion and roughly $48 billion in trading volume over the past day, Yahoo Finance data indicated.

Bitcoin price’s first move was a quick spike above $76,100, then a slide toward $75,600 before it settled back near $75,800. The range on the day has run from about $75,240 to $76,280, leaving the market little changed from its pre-release level even as rate futures repriced.

That muted finish fits the pattern after big macro prints. Most of the damage to the bitcoin price this week came from equity risk-off, not from the labor market itself, and a hot payrolls number mainly reinforces the path the Fed was already pricing.

CME FedWatch odds put roughly a 79% chance of a 25-basis-point hike at the Dec. 9 meeting after the release, up from about 70% into the open. The next employment report is due Dec. 4, five days before that decision, and the signal points the same way the last Fed hold did: stronger hiring and firmer wages leave less room for another pause.

Thursday’s session still sets the floor under the bitcoin price. The Nasdaq Composite fell about 5.5% and dropped into correction territory, more than 10% below its recent high, while the S&P 500 lost about 4.4% and the Dow shed roughly 1,800 points, or 3.4%, CNBC reported.

The Magnificent Seven erased more than $1 trillion in market value. Nvidia alone lost more than 9%, wiping out roughly $440 billion in capitalization, as investors priced in a slower AI data-center buildout after the Democratic midterm sweep.

Bitcoin has traded with that AI-heavy complex for most of the year, so a correction in the Nasdaq has pulled the bitcoin price lower even when on-chain flows looked steady. Miners that leased power and floor space to AI tenants are caught in both trades.

Fund flows followed the risk-off tape. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs posted $430.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, led by about $200 million from Fidelity’s FBTC and roughly $105 million from BlackRock’s IBIT, bringing week-to-date redemptions through Thursday to about $870 million.

Leverage kept the bitcoin price soft into the overnight. More than $1 billion in crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, mostly long bets built during Wednesday’s short-squeeze bounce that lifted bitcoin near $80,000.

Professional and business services led October hiring with 70,000 jobs, health care added 42,000, and transportation and warehousing gained 25,000. Manufacturing rose 19,000. The breadth undercuts the recent pattern in which health care carried most of the gains.

Policy risk has not eased either. House Democrats are already drafting their own market structure bill ahead of taking the committees in January, a backdrop that has hung over crypto equities since the midterm results first hit the bitcoin price.

Traders are watching whether the bitcoin price can defend the mid-$75,000 zone that held on election night and several times since August. A clean break would leave September’s low back in sight after a week that erased most of the post-election squeeze, now with a hotter labor market and firmer hike odds stacked on top.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $75,500.

#5192. Battery electric, solar-powered reefer units reach their first customers

The first regular production solar-powered electric trailer refrigeration unit (TRU) in Australia has hit the road with a real customer. Family-owned transport giant Wickham Freight Lines took delivery of the revolutionary reefer just this week.

Refrigerated trailers (reefers) are critical for hauling fresh food and flowers from the farm to your local market — the problem is that they pollute like crazy, powered by high sulfur, red-dye diesel-powered generators that run around the clock, even if the truck pulling them is fully electric. That constant diesel burn spews CO₂ and NOx into industrial and residential areas alike, and contributes significantly to fleets’ carbon emissions and noise pollution at a time when a number of municipalities are moving to regulate both.

It is, as they say, “a real problem,” but Sunswap Endurance cracked it with a solar-powered reefer that can keep food and pharmaceuticals cold in transit without burning a drop of diesel fuel – and now, they have real customers.

One of Australia’s largest privately owned trucking companies, Wickham Freight Lines decided to purchase the Sunswap Endurance solar trailer units following an initial trial, running from Sydney to Brisbane and back this past summer on a route covering a total of 1,670 km (~1,040 miles) over more than 30 hours of driving.

After Wickham’s drivers finished the round trip with over 65% of the battery’s capacity still left, the purchase was a no-brainer.

“I put the Sunswap Endurance into the fleet and told the team to stretch it — treat it as a normal unit,” explains Graham Keogh, director at Wickham Freight Lines. “We were genuinely impressed. I’m a true believer. I love the technology and I’m not a true tech person whatsoever. You can just see that it’s a winner … the performance, reliability and operational savings were beyond what we expected. For a business like ours, running a large fleet across the east coast, with a heavy focus on refrigerated and cold chain freight, this technology makes real commercial sense.”

Sunswap Endurance first showed their new solar reefer unit at HVIA TruckShowX in Hunter Valley this May past. It’s compelling, insultingly simple technology and I, for one, hope this customer delivery is just the first of many. Let us know what you think of these guys, in the comments.

Solar powered refrigeration truck looks like a natural combination. Cost of the solar is offset somewhat by not needing diesel tank, motor, and generator. Majority of savings probably come form not needing constant refueling (fuel + labor), or in not needing to overhaul the engine periodically.

I would be very interested in knowing how quickly the system pays for itself.

#5191. Trump-Backed American Bitcoin Turns a Profit as Bitcoin Rise Lifts Books

Trump-Backed American Bitcoin Turns a Profit as Bitcoin Rise Lifts Books

American Bitcoin, the Trump-family-backed mining venture, swung to a roughly $126 million profit in the third quarter, a sharp reversal from the loss it posted three months earlier, as a jump in Bitcoin’s book value and disciplined accumulation powered its best showing yet.

The Nasdaq-listed company, which trades under the ticker ABTC and is majority-owned by miner Hut 8, reported net income of about $126 million for the quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with a $57.2 million net loss in the second quarter.

Adjusted EBITDA came in at roughly $221 million, versus a $45 million loss the prior quarter. Much of the swing came from a $197 million gain on its digital assets, as Bitcoin’s value rose during the period.

The company grew its Bitcoin reserve to about 8,427 BTC, up roughly 425 coins, or 5%, from the end of June, and lifted its “satoshis per share,” a measure of how much Bitcoin backs each share, by about 5%. Notably, it did so without buying any Bitcoin or issuing new shares through its at-the-market program during the quarter, relying instead on its own mining output.

“These are significant results, particularly in a challenging digital asset market,” said Eric Trump, the company’s co-founder and chief strategy officer, touting the profit and the growth in Bitcoin per share. CEO Mike Ho credited “a disciplined, Bitcoin-denominated operating business,” saying the firm continued “mining Bitcoin at a structural discount to spot.”

The mining operation itself had a tougher quarter. American Bitcoin mined about 891 BTC, down from 932 in the prior period, and its cost to mine rose to roughly $42,100 per Bitcoin from $36,500, which it attributed to higher seasonal power costs. Gross margin slipped to 41% from 49%. The company ended the quarter with a fleet of about 89,242 miners and 28.1 exahashes per second of capacity.

The results cap a volatile stretch for the venture. American Bitcoin notched a record mining quarter earlier this year as it crossed 8,000 BTC, but its stock has struggled, hitting new lows and prompting a 1-for-15 reverse stock split in July. Shares have fallen well below their debut price since the company went public through a merger with Gryphon Digital Mining.

The company’s profit, while striking, rests heavily on an accounting gain tied to Bitcoin’s price rather than core mining operations, meaning a downturn in Bitcoin could just as easily swing the figure back into the red.

#5189. IREN’s bitcoin mining revenue drops 58% as firm plans full exit by year-end

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

IREN has been shifting its focus to the AI infrastructure business, and its AI cloud revenue rose 182% in the past quarter to $200 million.

IREN (IREN) reported bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining revenue of $28 million in the three months ended Sept. 30, which is a 58% quarter-over-quarter decline from $67 million and an 88% drop from $233 million a year earlier.

According to the company's 10-Q filing, IREN mined 402 BTC during the period, compared with 2,039 BTC in the same quarter last year. Average operating hashrate fell to 8.8 EH/s from 45.3 EH/s year-over-year. IREN held no bitcoin on its balance sheet as of the end of September as it liquidates mined bitcoin daily.

"We continue to operate Bitcoin miners at certain of our data centers while we transition this data center capacity toward AI Cloud," IREN wrote in the filing. "We aim to substantially complete this transition by December 31, 2026, with immaterial Bitcoin mining operations expected to continue through calendar year 2027."

IREN, which launched in 2018, announced in March last year that it will stop expanding its bitcoin mining operations and shift its focus to AI and high-performance computing through AI cloud services and data center businesses. Competing bitcoin miner firms MARA and CleanSpark have also turned to the AI infrastructure business.

Consequently, the company's quarterly revenue rose 66% sequentially to $228 million, driven by AI cloud revenue of around $200 million, which is up 182% from the prior quarter. The company posted a net loss of $263 million, narrower than the $684 million loss in the previous quarter, while adjusted EBITDA rose to $66 million from $19 million.

IREN said it has $20.6 billion in total assets, including $6 billion of data center assets, with assets and revenue underpinned by customer contracts with Nvidia, Microsoft, and 13 other customers.

The firm's Nasdaq-listed shares fell 17% on Thursday to close at $18.03, in line with a broader sell-off across AI and tech stocks.

Meanwhile, analysts at Bernstein gave IREN an Outperform rating on Friday, raising the price target to $55. They cited the firm’s “self-owned, unencumbered” physical power assets and the flexibility to decide its own pace of development.

#5187. Binance Hackers Swap About $50 Million of Stolen XRP Mostly Into Bitcoin, Elliptic Says

Hackers tied to the record Binance theft have begun converting a large slice of the stolen XRP into bitcoin, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said in an update dated through Friday, with about 42 million XRP — roughly $50 million — already swapped, mostly through THORChain.

Converted bitcoin from other stolen assets nearly doubled overnight to about 1,650 BTC, worth some $124 million, Elliptic wrote. Most of that jump came from the XRP route. About 192 million XRP, roughly $226 million, remained unswapped in addresses linked to the Binance theft.

The XRP was part of the Oct. 29 drain that Binance later put at $6.34 billion. Between 20:05 and 20:09 UTC that day, five hot wallets sent 233.46 million XRP to an attacker address. The FBI has attributed the campaign to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group. Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion while covering customer losses from its own funds.

The stolen XRP sat quiet at first. Early on Nov. 2, between 02:12 and 04:00 UTC, all about 233 million XRP moved into six new addresses, where it stayed until Nov. 5, Elliptic said. From the afternoon of Nov. 5, two of those six addresses began sending XRP on to dozens of new accounts that swapped it, mostly through THORChain into bitcoin.

A small amount of ether also went through THORChain into bitcoin that evening. The main bitcoin and ether holdings from the theft — the largest pools — have still not moved, Elliptic said. That pattern matches what the firm saw after the Bybit hack: turn freezable and mid-liquidity tokens into bitcoin first, then attempt cash-out later through OTC brokers.

The broader laundering trail is already vast. Since the Binance theft, Elliptic has counted more than 20,000 wallets on 16 blockchains, at least 36 services and at least 65,000 transactions — roughly one new wallet every 30 seconds and one transaction every 10 seconds. About $3.11 billion remains in addresses linked to the Binance theft, mostly as ether and bitcoin. A negligible amount has likely been cashed out so far.

Issuers can freeze many stablecoins. They cannot freeze XRP the same way, and they cannot freeze bitcoin. After Tether and Circle locked down large stablecoin tranches on Oct. 29, the attacker prioritized assets that sit outside that kill switch. Swapping XRP into bitcoin continues that logic: bitcoin is harder to claw back once it is fragmented across fresh addresses.

All of the stolen SOL, about $87 million at Elliptic’s prices, has already been swapped, bridged or mixed, with much of it ending as bitcoin. Most early Tron laundering — about $220 million in TRX — has also run, a flow Bitcoin Magazine noted when covering the quiet shutdown of the T3 Financial Crime Unit before the hack. Only small parts of the main ether and NEAR piles have moved.

U.S. bank supervisors have already checked in with lenders after the Binance breach. For bitcoin markets, the nearer question is how much more of the remaining ~192 million XRP — and eventually the parked ether — gets turned into BTC before anyone tries to cash out.

The test now is whether THORChain operators and other bridges keep blocking the published attacker addresses fast enough to slow the XRP-to-bitcoin pipe, or whether the rest of that pile follows the same overnight path the first $50 million already took.

#5186. XCMG debuts new, fully autonomous electric terminal tractor [video]

The new XCMG SDA411 concept terminal truck is fully electric, has no cabin, and an L4 autonomous tech stack that’s purpose-built for unmanned logistics – and, if XCMG get their way, it looks like the future of drayage truck operations.

Whether you call them terminal trucks, yard dogs, or anything else, these heavy-duty trucks do work that’s ideally suited to electrification. It’s all low-speed, high-torque, short distance, precision operation, all the time. And the quiet, vibration-free, instant power operation of electric drive is in its element.

Chinese equipment brand XCMG gets it, and they’re taking the electric terminal truck concept a step further with the debut of an all-new, cab-less design that’s 100% autonomous.

“With no cab and an L4 autonomous driving system, the platform is purpose-built for unmanned logistics,” said XCMG, on LinkedIn. “Its 360° perception system enables autonomous navigation, obstacle avoidance, task execution and parking, supporting continuous mixed-traffic operations in demanding environments.”

That same post included a video showing the SDA411 make its way around XCMG’s campus, making some emergency stops, and pulling up to a charger for a human attendant to plug it in (I guess they haven’t heard about Fortescue’s trick with the KUKA welding robot, yet). You can check out the post for yourself, below.

As for the autonomous aspect, while I generally side with labor and humanity over management and technology, there’s some truth to the industry claims that young people don’t want to drive trucks anymore, even with the promise of six-figure salaries.

Combine that with an aging workforce and not enough new talent entering the field, and virtually every industrial sector is struggling with a serious operator shortage. Autonomous equipment like the SDA411 could help address that shortage, keeping goods moving even when there aren’t enough drivers to go around. And rather than simply replacing workers, the technology could let experienced operators oversee multiple vehicles, take on supervisory roles and stay in the industry longer, while making these jobs accessible to people who might not otherwise be able to do them.

It doesn’t have to be autonomous because it’s electric it only needs to be powered by electric motors not gas to be better in every way.

If XCMG get their way, we’ll get to see how that all plays out somewhat sooner than later. In the meantime, we’ll have to settle for hearing what you think about the SDA411 and how it could impact brands like Kalmar and Orange EV in the comments.

#5184. Zcash’s major network upgrade NU7 goes live on mainnet, cutting block time by threefold

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NU7 also activates the Network Sustainability Mechanism, which removes 60% of each block's transaction fees from circulation and stores them in a public reserve.

Zcash Zcash (ZEC) announced that its major network upgrade, NU7, activated on the mainnet at block height 3,543,405 on Nov. 5.

The upgrade reduces the target block interval from 75 seconds to 25 seconds. Block subsidies are scaled down by the same factor so that the rate of new ZEC issuance remains essentially unchanged. The number of blocks between halvings is tripled, which preserves the existing halving schedule and the 21 million ZEC supply cap.

NU7 also activates the Network Sustainability Mechanism. The mechanism removes 60% of each block's transaction fees from circulation and stores it in a public reserve, with miners receiving the remaining 40%. The reserved fees are scheduled for gradual reissuance through block rewards beginning at block 8,939,664, expected to start in 2031.

"Like Bitcoin, Zcash faces a long-term challenge: how to sustain network security as block rewards decline. The NSM helps address that while preserving the 21 million coin cap," said Shielded Labs, an independent organization supporting the development of Zcash. The organization said NSM is its first major mainnet deployment on Zcash.

The upgrade also disallows version 4 transactions. As the original Sprout shielded pool relies exclusively on that format, any ZEC remaining in Sprout becomes unspendable. Approximately 12,224 ZEC sat in the pool at the activation height.

Node operators were required to upgrade software before the activation height to remain on the consensus chain. The changes do not require significant updates for most wallets.

ZEC dropped 5.17% in the past 24 hours to trade at $1,254.7 as of 2:05 a.m. on Friday, amid a broader downturn in the crypto market. The GMCI 30 index, measuring the performance of the top 30 cryptocurrencies, is down 6.9%.

#5182. OpenAI Found 948 Linux Security Flaws—And Figured Out How to Exploit Them

OpenAI Found 948 Linux Security Flaws—And Figured Out How to Exploit Them

OpenAI says its autonomous security agents discovered 948 distinct vulnerabilities in the Linux kernel, developed working exploits and drafted fixes for review—all for roughly $1.2 million in API credits.

“We chose Linux to study how effectively frontier models could find vulnerabilities and demonstrate their security impact in a codebase of this scale and importance,” OpenAI said in results published Wednesday.

The Linux kernel powers cloud servers, Android devices and much of the world's critical computing infrastructure. The flaws identified in OpenAI's experiment were local privilege-escalation vulnerabilities: An attacker would first need limited access to a vulnerable machine, but could then potentially obtain root privileges and take full control.

OpenAI's agents scanned about 2.8 million lines of kernel code, or roughly 7% of the project. “Our prototype identified and analyzed 2,308 candidate Linux kernel findings, dynamically reproduced 1,724 vulnerabilities, and produced 948 local privilege escalation (LPE) exploits that could allow an unprivileged local user to gain root access” OpenAI said.

After grouping exploits by their underlying cause and proposed fix, researchers counted 948 distinct flaws.

The October run cost $1,213,430 in API credits, equivalent to around $1,279 per vulnerability. OpenAI compared that with historical $20,000-to-$70,000 payouts for Linux privilege-escalation exploits at hacking competitions, suggesting a hypothetical value of $18.9 million to $66.3 million if the same results were found manually.

An earlier GPT-5.6 Sol campaign found 166 unique privilege-escalation vulnerabilities in July for $513,690. A newer, unreleased cyber model found more than five times as many in October, even after the kernel had undergone months of fixes.

OpenAI also said its agents used at least 10 exploitation techniques unfamiliar to the researchers and outside experts they consulted—not merely existing tricks retrieved from training data.

AI companies finding critical bugs are just another proof of how systems are capable of performing advanced cybersecurity research achieving results that previously required significant human expertise.

Last week, Anthropic disclosed that an unreleased version of Claude had discovered and exploited serious vulnerabilities in widely used software, including Chrome and Linux. Some of those exploits could execute malicious code remotely with little or no user interaction.

The economics of this research were already changing before OpenAI's announcement.

Researchers at Vidoc Security reproduced several vulnerability discoveries associated with Anthropic's powerful Mythos AI using publicly accessible models for less than $30 per file.

Those tests showed that finding vulnerabilities was becoming increasingly accessible, although reproducing a flaw is different from developing a reliable exploit capable of compromising a protected system.

OpenAI's latest experiment goes further by automating much of that exploitation process, creating a potential advantage for defenders trying to patch software before criminals can take advantage of the same weaknesses.

The company warned that attackers with limited access to an outdated operating system may increasingly be able to escalate their privileges with AI assistance.

OpenAI is attempting to restrict access to its more capable cybersecurity models through verified security programs. Its Daybreak Red initiative provides approved defenders with access to specialized AI tools for vulnerability research, exploit testing, and mitigation development.

#5181. Rule to Allow Crypto in 401(k)s Arrives at White House for Final Review

A final DOL rule that would ease alternative assets such as private equity, real estate and cryptocurrency into 401(k) retirement plans arrived at the White House on Thursday for review, Bloomberg Law reported, a procedural step that usually precedes public release.

The Office of Information and Regulatory Affairs logged the package on Nov. 5 under the title “Fiduciary Duties In Selecting Designated Investment Alternatives,” RIN 1210-AC38. The filing lists the DOL’s Employee Benefits Security Administration as the authoring office, marks the stage as a final rule, flags it as economically significant and notes that there is no legal deadline for completion.

The DOL unveiled the proposal on March 30. In that draft, plan fiduciaries who follow a documented process when adding private assets to a 401(k) menu would get a legal safe harbor under the Employee Retirement Income Security Act of 1974.

The six factors named in the proposal are performance, fees, liquidity, valuation, performance benchmarks and complexity. Satisfying them does not force plans to offer crypto. It lowers the litigation risk for employers that decide they want to.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 The White House is now reviewing a new rule which will allow Americans to invest in crypto through their 401(k) retirement plans 👀 pic.twitter.com/uXGGIXEzdC

President Donald Trump’s Aug. 7, 2025, executive order, “Democratizing Access to Alternative Assets for 401(k) Investors,” directed the Labor secretary to reexamine ERISA guidance and, where appropriate, propose rules with “appropriately calibrated safe harbors.” The order defined alternative assets to include private-market equity and debt, real estate, holdings in actively managed vehicles that invest in digital assets, commodities, infrastructure and lifetime-income strategies.

The March proposal implements that order. A DOL fact sheet said prudence under ERISA is process-based and that fiduciaries retain discretion to select any designated investment alternative if they follow the outlined review. Deputy Labor Secretary Keith Sonderling cast the approach as neutral: “The department’s days of picking winners and losers are over.”

That stance reverses a prior chill. In May 2025 the DOL rescinded its March 2022 compliance release that told fiduciaries to use “extreme care” before putting cryptocurrency on a 401(k) menu. The department said that phrase was not found in ERISA and restored a context-specific standard.

Later, it was reported that Sens. Bernie Sanders and Elizabeth Warren pressed the DOL to abandon the crypto-friendly path. House Republicans have separately pushed to codify Trump’s crypto order in statute.

OIRA review under Executive Order 12866 is the last interagency checkpoint before a final rule can be published in the Federal Register. Bloomberg Law said the arrival signals the package is nearing completion. The same office cleared the proposed version in late March before the DOL published it; that earlier pass took roughly two months after the January submission, per prior Bloomberg coverage of the roughly $12 trillion 401(k) market.

What is not yet public is the final text itself. Whether the six-factor safe harbor survived comment intact, how digital-asset vehicles are described, and what effective date the DOL chose will only be clear when OIRA returns the rule and the department posts it. Comment volume on the proposal was heavy; industry and labor groups have argued opposite sides of the prudence standard for months.

For bitcoin, the stakes sit in plan menus rather than spot ETF flows. Trump’s August order helped frame digital assets as a retirement-access issue for more than 90 million defined-contribution savers. A finalized DOL safe harbor would not put bitcoin and crypto on every lineup overnight. It would give plan committees a clearer map if they want exposure through an asset-allocation sleeve that holds digital assets.

The test now is how fast OIRA clears the economically significant package — and whether the published final rule still gives fiduciaries the process cover the March draft promised, or whether the comment fight rewrote the safe harbor before the White House stamps it.

#5180. Amflow launches two powerful new electric bikes, including its first SUV-style e-bike

Amflow, the electric bicycle brand that burst onto the scene with some of the most powerful and lightweight electric mountain bikes on the market, is expanding its lineup with two major releases. The company has announced global availability of its new Amflow TL Carbon, while also bringing its removable-battery Amflow PR Carbon series to the US for the first time.

Both models are powered by the latest Avinox mid-drive motor systems, which have quickly earned a reputation for delivering impressive power in surprisingly compact packages.

The biggest departure from Amflow’s roots is the new TL Carbon, which takes the company’s high-performance mountain biking DNA and applies it to a more versatile SUV-style electric bike. While less common in the US (ironically), SUV-style e-bikes are essentially a hybrid electric bicycle designed to act as an all-in-one, “do-it-all” vehicle combining the rugged build and off-road capability of an electric mountain bike with the practical, everyday utility of a city or trekking bike.

Rather than focusing exclusively on technical trails and aggressive mountain biking, the TL Carbon is designed to handle everything from daily commuting to touring and off-road adventures. And with Avinox’s RS800 battery featuring 800Wh of capacity, it’s got plenty of energy on board for diverse adventures.

The full-suspension bike uses the Avinox M2 motor, delivering up to 125 Nm of torque, which should provide more than enough grunt for steep climbs, heavy loads, and just about anything else riders can throw at it.

Avinox e-bike drive systems are known for many things, but chief among them is the extremely high power and torque they offer, eclipsing most other mid-drive motor systems on the market.

Despite that power and versatility, the TL Carbon weighs as little as 22.6 kg (49.8 lb). It also boasts an impressive maximum total weight capacity of 200 kg (441 lb), including the bike itself, rider, and cargo carried on its front and rear racks.

That’s a compelling combination for riders who want the performance of a premium electric mountain bike but need something practical enough to replace a car for everyday trips.

And perhaps most surprisingly, the price isn’t completely outrageous. The Amflow TL Carbon starts at $3,899 in the US and €3,499 in Germany, positioning it competitively against premium European trekking and SUV-style e-bikes.

Meanwhile, American riders are finally getting access to the Amflow PR Carbon series, which had previously launched in other markets.

Unlike Amflow’s earlier electric mountain bikes, the PR series features a removable battery, addressing one of the biggest practical complaints about high-performance eMTBs with integrated batteries.

The new 800 Wh battery weighs just 4 kg (8.8 lb) and can be removed for convenient indoor charging or swapped for a fresh battery to extend a ride. That’s especially useful for riders who don’t have a convenient charging outlet near their bike storage area.

Amflow claims up to 158 km (98 miles) of range under ideal conditions, though that’s based on relatively gentle riding on flat pavement in Eco mode. Riders tackling technical trails with high assistance levels should expect considerably less. But any way you slice it, 800 Wh is a huge battery for eMTBs and should deliver plenty of range under most conditions.

The standard PR Carbon uses the Avinox M2 motor with up to 125 Nm of torque, while the higher-end PR Carbon Pro upgrades to the more powerful Avinox M2S motor, producing up to 150 Nm of torque.

In the US, the PR Carbon starts at $4,999, while the PR Carbon Pro commands $6,799. European pricing starts at €4,499 and €5,899, respectively, based on German pricing.

All three bikes are available beginning today through ⁠Amflow’s official website and its network of authorized dealers. In the US, both models generally arrive as Class 1 e-bikes (no throttle) with a top speed of 20 mph (32 km/h), though Amflow models can typically be unlocked to Class 3 performance with a top speed of 28 mph (45 km/h).

Amflow has been one of the more interesting brands to watch in the premium e-bike space, largely because of how aggressively it has pushed the boundaries of motor performance and bike weight.

But these latest releases show that the company is thinking beyond simply building increasingly powerful electric mountain bikes.

The TL Carbon is particularly interesting to me because it brings that impressive Avinox performance to a more practical platform. As someone who spends a minority of my riding time in the dirt, Amflow suddenly looks a lot more interesting to me, personally. Not everyone needs a dedicated eMTB, but plenty of riders would appreciate a lightweight, powerful, well-equipped electric bike that can handle both weekday commuting and weekend adventures.

And the PR Carbon’s removable battery is a welcome development, especially as the industry continues to recognize that convenience and serviceability matter just as much as outright performance.

With the Avinox ecosystem continuing to expand across more bike brands and categories, Amflow is also facing increasing competition from other manufacturers using the same impressive motors. That makes it all the more important for the brand to differentiate itself through thoughtful bike design, not just headline-grabbing power figures.

#5178. Kalshi hit with injunction over sports contracts on California tribal lands

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of two California tribes.

In a Wednesday order, U.S. District Judge Jacqueline Scott Corley ruled that the injunction covers contracts tied to sports including football, basketball, baseball, soccer, tennis, and Ultimate Fighting Championship events.

The tribes — Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians — must provide boundary maps by Nov. 13, the judge said.

Kalshi is required to report the status of the steps it has taken to comply with the injunction within 21 days, according to the order.

Sports and gaming attorney Daniel Wallach described the order as the first preliminary injunction secured by tribes against Kalshi nationwide, calling it "a major development in tribal gaming litigation."

The tribes filed the lawsuit in July 2025, alleging that Kalshi's sports event contracts constitute unauthorized Class III gaming under the Indian Gaming Regulatory Act (IGRA) and violate the tribes' gaming ordinances.

Kalshi has argued that its sports event contracts are federally regulated swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act (CEA), and that the CEA's grant of exclusive jurisdiction to the Commodity Futures Trading Commission preempts tribal gaming law.

The judge initially denied the tribes' motion for a preliminary injunction in November 2025. The tribes then appealed, and a Ninth Circuit panel partially reversed the decision in September.

"Reversing the district court's denial of a preliminary injunction in part, the panel held that the tribes were likely to succeed in their claims that Kalshi's sports event contracts on tribal lands violated IGRA and the tribes' gaming ordinances," the panel wrote.

The Ninth Circuit panel also held that the CEA's "exclusive" jurisdiction did not extend to Kalshi's sports event contracts.

The Block has reached out to Kalshi for comment.

Kalshi faces mounting legal challenges, with more states taking action against the prediction markets platform.

On Wednesday, Arkansas Attorney General Tim Griffin sued Kalshi for allegedly operating an illegal gambling platform in the state.

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said, adding that such practice is "squarely prohibited" by Arkansas law.

Last week, Utah also filed criminal charges against Kalshi and two company executives, accusing them of illegally promoting online gambling. Utah has also sued Kalshi and two Susquehanna trading firms for allegedly operating an illegal gambling business.

#5177. Zcash Upgrade Could Lock $16 Million in Crypto Forever

Zcash Upgrade Could Lock $16 Million in Crypto Forever

At recent market prices, those holdings represent roughly $15 million to $16 million that could become effectively frozen when the network activates its NU7 upgrade.

“NU7 (block 3,543,405) makes v4 transactions invalid, so what remains in Sprout is unspendable: frozen, not burned,” Zecstats says in its information card for Sprout.

The upgrade is scheduled for block 3,543,405, expected Thursday evening in the United States. The exact deadline depends on how quickly the blockchain processes new blocks, rather than a predetermined clock time.

Unlike Bitcoin, where transactions are generally visible to anyone examining the blockchain, Zcash allows users to conceal transaction amounts and the addresses involved. It accomplishes this through technology called zero-knowledge proofs, which can verify that a transaction is valid without revealing its private details.

NU7 changes that by eliminating version-4 transactions, the only format capable of moving coins out of Sprout. According to the network's technical specification, transactions using that format will become invalid after activation.

The decision addresses a problem familiar to anyone maintaining old software: supporting obsolete technology creates additional complexity and potential security vulnerabilities.

Developers haven't technically destroyed the coins. They will continue counting toward Zcash's circulating issuance calculations, and a future network upgrade could theoretically restore access, but that is not guaranteed, and the team has not shown any interest in doing so.

In fact, the specification explicitly warns that spending those funds may never be re-enabled.

Legacy holders are the people most exposed to the change. Anyone whose ZEC remains in Sprout must move it through a compatible withdrawal mechanism before the upgrade activates, or risk losing access indefinitely.

Sprout addresses begin with "zc," which can help users recognize old wallet records. Regular ZEC holders using modern supported wallets do not need to migrate their coins out of Sprout, although keeping wallet software updated remains important.

The blockchain also cannot reveal who owns the remaining Sprout balance, meaning nobody knows how much belongs to active users and how much is already inaccessible because its owners lost their keys.

Meanwhile, NU7 brings a benefit for everyone else: Zcash's target block interval falls from 75 seconds to 25 seconds, meaning transactions should receive their first confirmation approximately three times faster, according to the Zcash Foundation.

For Sprout holders, however, the relevant number is block 3,543,405 which will happen later today. As of the time of publication, the latest available block is 3,543,027. Once the network reaches block 3,543,405, any remaining coins in the old pool will be frozen under the new rules.

#5175. Strive (ASST) Posts $474.8 Million Common Net Income as Bitcoin Yield Hits 63.2%

Strive reported $474.8 million of net income attributable to common stockholders for the third quarter ended Sept. 30, a print that arrived Friday alongside a company claim that Class A total return had outperformed bitcoin by 103.7% year to date through the same date.

GAAP net income was $509.0 million, or $5.24 a share on a fully diluted basis. Almost all of that came from a $533.7 million unrealized gain on digital assets marked to fair value. Operating revenue for the quarter was just $2.7 million, so the earnings line is a treasury mark, not a fee business.

In a post on X that led with the outperformance figure, Strive also highlighted a year-to-date bitcoin yield of 63.2% and a bitcoin dollar gain of $9.00 per assumed diluted share outstanding, both measured through Sept. 30. Chairman and CEO Matthew Cole tied those metrics to the stock’s relative showing against the asset.

“Strive has continued to successfully execute on its treasury management strategy, delivering a strong bitcoin yield year to date in 2026, which I believe is the primary driver of our common equity’s outperformance relative to Bitcoin so far this year,” Cole said in the earnings release.

Bitcoin yield, as Strive defines it, is the percentage change in bitcoin per share over a period — not a cash yield. On that measure the company posted 18.5% in the third quarter alone and 19.3% from Oct. 1 through Oct. 30, after the SATS preferred debut.

Strive acquired 8,136 bitcoin in the third quarter at an average cost of about $78,885 and 20,373 bitcoin over the nine months ended Sept. 30. Another 15,124 coins followed between Oct. 1 and Oct. 30. Holdings stood at 28,000 bitcoin on Sept. 30 and 43,124 by Oct. 30, keeping Strive among the largest public corporate stacks after recent multi-thousand-coin buys and smaller weekly adds that still trailed Strategy.

Digital assets at fair value were $2.34 billion on the Sept. 30 balance sheet. Cash had risen to $490.4 million by Oct. 30, and Strive still held Strategy’s variable-rate STRC preferred with a fair value of about $50.1 million.

The capital stack that funded the buys is mostly perpetual preferreds. On Oct. 20 Strive closed a registered offering of 7 million shares of its 15% Series A perpetual preferred (SATS) for about $609 million in net proceeds. Preferred dividends of $34.2 million in the quarter are what turned the $509.0 million GAAP profit into the $474.8 million common figure.

Strip the story to nine-month GAAP and the picture flips. For the nine months ended Sept. 30, Strive posted a net loss attributable to common stockholders of $88.4 million. The third-quarter profit is real under fair-value accounting, but it is concentrated in one mark-to-market line.

ASST itself has not moved in a straight line with the company’s bitcoin-yield scoreboard. Shares closed at $20.59 on Thursday, down about 10% on the day, and traded near $20.70 early Friday. That leaves the stock roughly 29% below its level a year earlier, when Yahoo Finance data put the close near $29, and about 33% under its Oct. 21 local high of $30.75.

The 52-week range on Yahoo Finance runs from $7.02 to $34.40. Year to date, the price path from the Jan. 2 close near $17.30 into the mid-$20s is a gain of roughly 20%, with a February low near the 52-week floor and a September push back toward $30. Through Sept. 30 — the cutoff on Strive’s outperformance claim — ASST had risen about 70% from that January close while bitcoin was down roughly 7% over the same window on Yahoo Finance data, a wide gap even before the company’s own total-return methodology.

The longer chart still carries the scar of 2025, when Asset Entities became the public shell for Strive’s bitcoin-treasury reverse merger and the share price spiked into triple digits on weekly closes before collapsing. Investors who held through that conversion saw a different ride than the tidy year-to-date relative-return figure Strive put at the top of Friday’s note.

Cole said the recent SATS issuance leaves Strive “never been in a better position to generate long-term returns in excess of Bitcoin for our common equity shareholders.” The test for ASST from here is whether another quarter of bitcoin yield and preferred-funded buys can reopen the gap to bitcoin after a year in which the stock still sits well below where it traded twelve months ago, even as the treasury has climbed past 30,000 then 40,000 bitcoin.

#5174. All aboard! Massachusetts is getting 10 new electric transit trains

The Massachusetts Bay Transportation Authority (MBTA) board of directors voted unanimously Thursday to approve the purchase of a new generation of battery-powered public transit trains – and they can’t arrive soon enough.

“(MBTA) has, obviously, an immediate need for locomotives,” explains William Wolfgang, the MBTA’s senior director of vehicle engineering. The new machines will replace the oldest locomotives in the MBTA fleet, GP40s built more than 50 years ago and F40s that are well over 30 years old. In a presentation to the MTBA board, MTBA said 13 of the GP40s have, “exceeded their useful life,” while the F40s will reach the end of their life between 2029 and 2035.

The MBTA will put in an order for 20 Siemens-built “Charger” locomotives; 10 Tier 4 diesel-powered hybrid electric units and 10 dual-mode battery/catenary electric units – and, beyond trackside power for charging the batteries, there are other challenges presented by going electric, most notably using existing cab cars to control the battery-electric units. “We’re going to have to work with railroad operations, engineering, and the Brotherhood of [Locomotive Engineers and Trainmen] to try and determine the best way to handle that,” adds Wolfgang. “That will be an upcoming change order once we get into the design review process.”

The total bill comes out to $310.4 million. A combination of state and federal funding will pay for the order, including a recent $200 million transfer from the Massachusetts Department of Transportation.

The contract includes an option for additional locomotives, although details were not discussed during the meeting. Delivery of the new Siemens Charger trains is set to begin in 2029, with all 20 set to be in service by the end of 2030. Siemens has delivered 440 Charger model hybrid- and battery-electric locomotives globally at the time of this writing.

#5172. Nigeria’s central bank introduces stablecoin licensing rules, bans algorithmic versions

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The rules took effect on Nov. 5 and cover virtual asset activities conducted in Nigeria, directed at Nigerian users, or affecting the country's financial system.

The Central Bank of Nigeria has introduced new licensing requirements for stablecoin issuers and other non-security virtual asset service providers, according to regulations that took effect on Thursday.

According to a circular issued to relevant stakeholders, the regulations establish separate licensing categories for naira-referenced and foreign-currency-referenced stablecoins. Issuers must also secure approval for each token before beginning issuance.

The central bank said the rules cover activities carried out in Nigeria or affecting the country's payments system, financial sector, or foreign exchange market, even if the operator has no physical presence in the country.

Under the framework, foreign-currency stablecoins can only be offered, distributed, exchanged, or otherwise made available to Nigerians as Class 2 tokens issued through a Nigerian-incorporated entity holding a Foreign Currency Issuer Licence.

The CBN also requires issuers to obtain written approval for each token, covering its classification, reference currency, reserve arrangements, name, ticker, and supported blockchain networks, per the circular.

The rules also enforce compliance with anti-money laundering and counter-terrorist financing obligations. Entities must implement customer due diligence, sanctions screening, transaction monitoring, and Travel Rule compliance across all custodial wallets, peer-to-peer services, and remittance channels.

Meanwhile, some tokens are prohibited under the rules, including algorithmic stablecoins. The central bank said the ban covers tokens that rely primarily on market dynamics, rather than eligible reserve assets, to maintain their value.

The framework gives the CBN authority over stablecoins and virtual assets used for payments, transfers, remittances, foreign exchange, and settlement. The Securities and Exchange Commission retains oversight of securities and capital-market activities.

Companies operating across both areas must obtain the relevant approvals from each regulator. The regulations took effect on Nov. 5, 2026, with existing operators given 180 days to apply for the appropriate licence or authorization, subject to transitional requirements.

#5171. Mercado Bitcoin Said Its Hack Was Contained, But Blockchain Data Tells a Different Story

Mercado Bitcoin Said Its Hack Was Contained, But Blockchain Data Tells a Different Story

An attacker drained Mercado Bitcoin-linked deposit addresses nearly four hours after the Brazilian exchange said it had contained a security breach, new blockchain research shows.

Bitquery's analysis, updated Thursday, traces more than $48 million in stolen assets across 11 blockchains. Its findings challenge the exchange's claim that it stopped the attack quickly.

The Bitcoin exchange announced at 9:56 a.m. Brazil time on November 2 that it had quickly contained the incident. Bitquery says 36,042 Ethereum customer deposit addresses were emptied between 9:27 a.m. and 1:56 p.m. Brazil time.

Those addresses held roughly $266,000, a small fraction of the total theft. But the attacker could still move those funds after the exchange’s announcement.

A customer deposit address works much like a personal receiving account for cryptocurrency. The exchange later gathers those coins into a shared online wallet, called a hot wallet, to process everyday transactions. Moving funds from a deposit address requires its private key, the secret that authorizes transfers.

Bitquery says the withdrawals were authorized directly by the deposit addresses, suggesting the attacker had access to their private keys or to a system capable of signing transactions on their behalf.

“That needs a key for each address, or a way into the system that signs for them,” the researchers explained. They warned that exchanges typically generate deposit keys from a single master key, meaning the attacker may still be able to access other customer addresses.

The amount stolen is also disputed. Initial estimates put the amount above $40 million, while Mercado Bitcoin's updated FAQ now puts affected assets at about $36.3 million. Bitquery estimates that $48.6 million was stolen across 11 blockchains.

Mercado Bitcoin's public updates have not explained the gap between its containment claim and the later transfers. The company assured customers it would cover user losses with its own funds.

Bitquery warns customers against reusing old deposit addresses until the exchange confirms they are safe. Mercado Bitcoin's reopening plan begins November 9 with balance viewing only; deposits, withdrawals and trading have no restart date.

#5169. Former FBI Agent Pleads Guilty to Stealing $2.47 Million in Crypto From Investigations

A former FBI agent pleaded guilty Thursday to stealing about $2.47 million in cryptocurrency with wallet credentials the bureau had collected in its own cases, the U.S. Attorney’s Office for the Eastern District of Virginia announced.

Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, of Ashburn, Virginia, entered pleas to wire fraud and money laundering in federal court in Alexandria. U.S. District Judge Patricia Tolliver Giles accepted the plea. He faces up to 20 years in prison and is scheduled for sentencing on March 4, 2027.

Court papers put the illicit haul at $2,474,380.66. Prosecutors say Yaroch made roughly 100 fraudulent transfers using credentials tied to 35 separate FBI investigations, drawing on wallets controlled by investigation subjects, adversarial nation-state actors and at least one wallet the FBI itself had created for an operation.

The Justice Department Office of the Inspector General had flagged the case in August after Yaroch’s arrest on a criminal complaint. That earlier account described a smaller set of transfers — about 10 moves totaling roughly $1 million — and noted that Yaroch self-reported the activity to two department employees on July 29, 2026. The plea figures are larger.

From 2017 through early 2025, Yaroch worked in the FBI Boston Division on a squad focused on an adversarial nation-state. From February 2025 through July 31, 2026, he served in the FBI’s Counterintelligence and Espionage Division at headquarters, with access to systems that stored cryptocurrency credentials gathered in investigations.

Beginning no later than December 2024, Yaroch used that access to move coins out of those wallets and into ones he controlled. After the transfers, he swapped the stolen cryptocurrency into other digital assets and ran further transactions meant to obscure its source, nature and ownership.

Between Nov. 25, 2025, and Jan. 13, 2026, he moved about $93,082 into one of his accounts, then shuffled funds among other accounts to cover debts, including credit cards, car payments and loans, the office said.

FOX 5 DC, citing the same U.S. Attorney’s Office release, noted the Portugal planning that appears in the plea papers. Yaroch asked ChatGPT how to maximize investment returns and where to live in the European Union. After the chatbot suggested buying a vineyard in Portugal, he gave Portuguese lawyers a power of attorney on June 15, 2026, to register him with the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority, obtain a tax identification number and collect the password to his finance portal. He arranged travel to Portugal for September 2026.

The agreed statement of facts goes beyond the dollar figure. Yaroch’s conduct compromised multiple FBI investigations into criminal groups and nation-state adversaries, the office said, and it compromised FBI sources and methods used in foreign intelligence collection and criminal cases. It also “deprived FBI employees of their ability to carry out their functions to protect public safety and national security.”

First Assistant U.S. Attorney Theophani K. Stamos and Daniel S. Wierzbicki, special agent in charge of the FBI Washington Field Office’s Counterintelligence Division, announced the plea. Assistant U.S. Attorneys Kathleen Robeson and Russell Carlberg are prosecuting, with help from the National Security Cyber Section of the Justice Department’s National Security Division and the DOJ inspector general’s Mid-Atlantic region. The case is 1:26-CR-208 in the Eastern District of Virginia.

The admission lands in a week when federal crypto-crime stories keep stacking up — from a failed bitcoin kidnapping plot that produced its own guilty plea last month to FBI tallies putting U.S. crypto fraud losses above $11 billion in 2025, a figure the White House has used to frame a wider crackdown.

What sentencing will do with the gap between Yaroch’s July self-report and the $2.47 million plea figure is still open. So is how many of the 35 FBI cases can be repaired after an insider treated the bureau’s own wallet credentials as a personal ATM. March 4, 2027, is when Judge Giles gets to answer the first question.

#5168. Range Energy shows how its eTrailer saves almost as much as a Tesla Semi

Range Energy, a company working to electrify trucking through the use of a trailer with its own battery and motors, has published an analysis showing how much fleets can save through electrification – and just at the right time, with diesel at record highs.

You may have heard a little something about diesel prices lately, which have just about doubled in less than a year due to Donald Trump’s war of choice in Iran.

Diesel is now at record highs, and it’s devastating fleets and truck drivers and anyone who uses any products that are delivered on trucks (so, everyone). It’s an urgent problem that needs an urgent solution, and solutions are not forthcoming from the responsible entities.

So, fleets have to seek another solution, and Range Energy would like to be the one that provides it.

Range is a startup based in California, founded and staffed by former Tesla employees and others from around the EV industry. It’s building an electrified trailer which we demoed at ACT Expo in 2024 (really, watch it, it’s a very cool demo) which promises to cut fleet fuel use significantly.

It does this by having its own battery and motors inside the trailer, meaning the tractor doesn’t have to work nearly as hard as the trailer can effectively pull its own weight. Even though the tractor still runs on diesel, it uses a whole lot less of it.

This differs from the other popular option for fleets: full electrification. There are a number of electric semi tractors available, and fleets have been moving to install them, getting their depots ready for charging. But Range lets you bring your own tractor, and electrifies the trailer instead.

Range previously published some early findings, showing a 36% reduction in fuel use in a testing scenario. It also did a pilot program with an egg farm in California, which improved the fuel efficiency of the farm’s diesel fleet by 50-70%.

Now Range has published its own white paper showing comparative savings that fleets can achieve with either its own solution, the eTrailer, or Tesla’s solution, the fully electric Tesla Semi. The paper does not cover the various other electric semi tractors available on the market today, just a face-off between Range and Tesla.

The analysis goes through several scenarios, mostly focusing on refrigerated trailer units (“reefers”), which is the first product Range is focusing on.

Trailer electrification is a great idea for reefers, because they usually fill up on volume rather than weight (called “cubing out”), meaning battery weight doesn’t eat into maximum trailer payload. Batteries also offer power to run refrigeration, meaning you don’t need to have a diesel generator on the trailer.

The upshot of the whole thing is that: fleets can save money with the Range eTrailer, and can theoretically save more money with the Tesla Semi, but the numbers are relatively close in the most common scenario, and the Tesla scenarios have a much wider spread depending on what implementation you consider.

For example, if you only use public chargers rather than your own depot, the Tesla Semi will be more costly because energy will be more expensive to your fleet than if you bought it at commercial utility rates. So, even though installing chargers in your depot sounds expensive, it will pencil out better in the end.

The best money savings came from having two Semis share the same charger, but that requires tight timings and overnight driving.

Another important feature Range points out is build time for a project. The nice thing about the eTrailer is it can slot into fleets immediately with little operational change required, whereas the larger charging needs of an electric tractor) tend to require bigger projects to get a depot ready with chargers (but despite the higher upfront costs, you get better long-term savings, since you avoid diesel entirely with an electric tractor).

In the end, Range found that its solution saves a little more money than the Tesla Semi does in most scenarios, but the difference is quite narrow, with each option having its strengths and weaknesses. Both solutions are better than diesel in most scenarios, but not all of them.

You don’t necessarily need to choose between the two, as an electrified trailer can still be used with an electric tractor. It works the same way in either case – using its own motors to take some of the effort off the tractor. This means extended range, for longer hauls with light loads that couldn’t quite make it on the Tesla Semi’s inherent range. And the benefit of being able to run the reefer while disconnected from the tractor or shore power remains as well.

The analysis has plenty of limitations. It has to estimate future diesel costs (it used a number below current prices, but still historically high), didn’t cover financing costs, it doesn’t have real-world data for the Tesla Semi, didn’t cover electrified tractor or trailer options from other companies, can’t analyze individual charging costs site-to-site since each is specific to a utility company, didn’t cover the myriad incentives available in various regions, and most of all it didn’t consider diesel emissions, which are a huge societal cost to everyone that electrification reduces (and full electrification reduces more).

So in the end, every fleet has a different use case, and should run the numbers themselves to see how either solution would fit in. But in the generalized case, it looks like there are plenty of savings available out there, whether through tractor or trailer electrification.

A good idea in theory but there are three trailers for every tractor-cab because trailers spend a lot of time sitting idle. For specific closed-loop customers and routes this can very very helpful but not as a general solution.

You know what can offer relief right now? Importing Class 8 Trucks. China is selling 280,000 heavy duty EV trucks this year. Trade barriers hurt economies.

Unfortunately, given diesel prices are at record highs right now, both of the options in the paper are a little suboptimal. Tesla Semis have been on order for almost ten years now, and Tesla only just claimed it has started production in earnest. And Range is still about a year out from volume production of its eTrailer, which it says will start in late 2027 – so, while an eTrailer will be able to slot into a fleet immediately when it’s available, it’s not available yet, which puts a damper on that potential benefit.

So neither can offer immediate relief, but both offer good options for fleets to plan for the next inevitable oil shock – which is why Range didn’t base its analysis solely on current prices (remember, we just had another shock in 2022, this nonsense happens all the time, maybe it’s time to stop using oil). But, the numbers in the paper for the Tesla Semi can be compared to numbers for other electric tractors already available today, which can in turn be compared to the costs of the eTrailer, for fleets so inclined to make that comparison.

#5166. Crypto ETFs see over half a billion dollars in outflows amid post-midterm market slide

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

U.S. spot crypto ETFs saw more than $500 million in combined net outflows on Thursday as crypto and tech markets fell across the board.

U.S. spot crypto exchange-traded funds witnessed more than half a billion dollars in net outflows on Thursday as markets reversed initial gains following the U.S. midterms election results.

Among bitcoin ETFs, Fidelity's FBTC led Thursday's net outflows with $199.9 million, followed by BlackRock's IBIT with $105 million and Grayscale's GBTC with $49.9 million, according to data compiled by The Block. Franklin Templeton's EZBC, VanEck's HODL, and Ark Invest's ARKB also saw net outflows, while the remaining ETFs posted zero flows for the day.

Ethereum funds also witnessed heavy net outflows, led by BlackRock's ETHA and ETHB, with $75.2 billion and $22 billion in redemptions, respectively. Fidelity's FETH and Grayscale's ETHE saw outflows too.

Other major crypto ETFs didn't fare much better, accounting for size. U.S. Solana ETF saw a combined $1.2 million in net outflows for the day, Zcash ETFs $11 million, and Hyperliquid ETFs $3 million, according to SoSoValue data. XRP ETFs were the only outlier, with zero flows for the day.

Major U.S. crypto ETFs have now seen a combined net outflow of more than $1.1 billion so far this week.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell 5.6% on Thursday, more than erasing an initial 4% gain on Wednesday following Tuesday's U.S. midterm elections. Ether Ethereum (ETH) was hit harder, falling 7.8% for the day, with the GMCI 30 index, representing a selection of the top 30 cryptocurrencies, dropping 6.4%.

Tech stocks also suffered, with the Nasdaq Composite falling 5.5%, and the S&P 500 dropping 4.4% as investors continue to weigh the impact of potential restrictions on the AI infrastructure buildout following the Democrats' sweep of both the House and Senate.

Kyle Rodda, senior financial market analyst at Capital.com, said the Democratic sweep added to concerns over the AI investment boom, with investors already questioning technology valuations and the pace of infrastructure spending.

"The regulatory and political risks arise at a time when the proverbial AI trade was already on shakier foundations," Rodda said, pointing to disappointing earnings and signs of slowing AI spending.

Axel Rudolph, chief technical analyst at IG, told The Block that bitcoin has been "dragged into the AI rout as investors rapidly unwind risk across technology and crypto markets."

Rudolph said bitcoin's correlation with technology stocks had left it exposed to the selloff, with more than $1 billion in leveraged crypto positions liquidated in 24 hours as the market continued to decline.

Friday's U.S. jobs report could complicate matters further for markets, particularly if the figures give the Federal Reserve more reason to raise rates.

Economists expect around 60,000 to 65,000 new jobs, according to Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com. She warned that a stronger jobs report, particularly one showing higher wage growth, could send both Treasury yields and the dollar higher.

Despite the broader market weakness, André Dragosch, head of research for Europe at Bitwise, expects bitcoin to outperform U.S. equities as the AI trade unwinds.

Dragosch said bitcoin is now more sensitive to the dollar and expectations for monetary policy than to economic growth, and could decouple from stocks if financial conditions improve.

#5163. Hypercall to launch first Anthropic-linked pre-IPO options market

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Trading is expected to begin Monday, with calls, puts and more complex strategies available in select non-US jurisdictions.

Hypercall is launching what it says is the first options market tied to Anthropic ahead of its IPO.

The onchain options platform expects trading to begin Monday, founder Jake Sylvestre and growth and marketing head Dan Edlebeck told The Block. The contracts will trade around the clock but will not be available to U.S. customers.

The options are synthetic, cash-settled derivatives. This means that traders will not own actual Anthropic shares or receive any voting rights when a contract expires. Payouts will be calculated on a contract's strike price and reference price at expiry.

The launch would add options to a market that already offers Anthropic-linked perpetual futures contracts. Sylvestre said Hypercall supports everything from basic calls and puts, which he called "vanillas," to more advanced spreads and strategies. "It's a fully featured options platform," he said. "Any kind of option you want to structure, we can do."

Hypercall is built on Hyperliquid, where existing Anthropic-linked perps give market makers a way to hedge the risk of selling options before the company's shares begin trading publicly. Edleback said that gives Hypercall a head start: "We're able to provide options on these assets before anyone else can," he said.

The platform already offers options tied to SpaceX and the S&P 500 as well as major cryptos like bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) and ether Ethereum (ETH), according to the executives. Sylvestre previously reported that Hypercall handled $536 million in options notional trading volume for September.

The rollout comes as traders become less confident that Anthropic will even go public this month or even before the end of 2026.

On Polymarket, the chances of a November IPO fell from roughly 88% Tuesday to as low as 51% by Thursday, while the probability of a January 2027 debut jumped from less than 1% to nearly 17%. The market had generated around $566,000 in trading volume.

A similar market on Kalshi, with nearly $4.9 million in volume, put the chances of an IPO before Dec. 1 at 60%, down from around 88%. Contracts on a debut before Jan. 1 and Feb. 1 traded at 63% and 85%, respectively.

Technology shares also sold off sharply on Thursday, with the Nasdaq falling around 6% during the session amid growing concerns around AI infrastructure spending.

Sylvestre said the market backdrop had changed his concerns about pricing the options, from a post-IPO rally to a potential slump.

"People that market make options are really pricing volatility," he said. "How fast it drops matters as much as how much it drops."

An option can expire worthless, but a price swing won't automatically trigger a margin liquidation like with trading perps on leverage.

Anthropic filed its S-1 on Oct. 26, with plans to list on the Nasdaq under the ticker ANT. The Claude developer reported $16.3 billion in first-half revenue and $9 billion in compute spending. Its prospectus revealed that its five largest customers accounted for around 25% of revenue through the end of September.

#5162. Volkswagen gets L3 autonomous driving test permit in China, eyes 2027

Volkswagen Group China now has a permit to test Level 3 autonomous driving on public roads in Hefei, and it says testing is already underway. The permit also covers large-scale driving data collection in the city.

It’s the latest step toward VW’s goal of L3 technology readiness in China by 2027. And the timing is good as the group holds its first China Technology Day in Hefei on Wednesday.

VW says the permit was issued under China’s strictest L3 framework and that its setup meets the country’s latest mandatory safety design standards. It didn’t say which authority issued it, how many vehicles are in the fleet, or which roads they can use.

Hefei makes sense as a base. It’s home to the Volkswagen China Technology Company (VCTC), the group’s largest R&D center outside Germany, and to its Volkswagen Anhui production site.

The software comes from CARIZON, VW’s intelligent driving unit in China, which started as a joint venture between CARIAD and Horizon Robotics. Its driver assistance system is called Hyper Sense 8 (HS8), which VW describes as its first “full-scenario” ADAS.

Behind it is GAIA 2.0, a world model VW built itself. VW says it runs simulation equal to 5 million km of safety testing per day, using about 30 million real-road scenario clips, and it wants to get that to 10 million km per day. VW is also developing its own system-on-chip, the C7H, which it says can support up to Level 4.

Ralf Brandstätter, chairman and CEO of Volkswagen Group China, kept the message on safety. He said the group “will never advance technology at the cost of safety” and called the permit an important step on the road to L3 readiness (our translation from Chinese).

This is a road test permit. VW can’t sell L3 cars to customers with it.

China’s MIIT granted the country’s first L3 product approvals in December 2025 to two sedans: a Changan model capped at 50 km/h on designated Chongqing expressways, and a BAIC Arcfox model capped at 80 km/h on three Beijing routes. GAC’s Hyptec and XPeng already hold L3 road test permits too. So VW isn’t leading here. It’s catching up to Chinese brands on their home turf.

In July, VW, CARIZON, and Horizon Robotics said they would deepen their partnership, giving CARIZON “white-box” access to Horizon’s AI foundation model, and targeted the start of L3 deliveries in the second half of 2027. VW’s L2++ system is rolling out on seven new NEV models from its Chinese joint ventures this year.

VW also leans on XPeng in China. It bought a roughly 5% stake in XPeng in 2023 and co-developed its China zonal electrical architecture, CEA, with the startup.

Outside China, the only Western automaker with a certified L3 system on sale is Mercedes-Benz, whose Drive Pilot works on approved highways in Germany and in California and Nevada. Mercedes also got approval to test Level 4 driving in Beijing back in 2024. Tesla’s FSD remains a Level 2 system everywhere it’s sold, China included.

L3 is where liability moves. When the system is engaged, the automaker is responsible for the driving, not the person in the seat. That’s why so few companies have shipped it, and why the ones that have keep it on a short leash: specific roads, low speeds, mostly traffic jams.

China has been serious about building a framework for automakers to bring this techonology to market, and through it, we are seeing who is interested in this version of autonomy.

Not everyone is. Many automakers, including Tesla, have been talking about “skipping” level 3 and going straight to level 4.
