#5113. Asian crypto exchanges batten down the hatches as hacks surge: Asia Express

Asian crypto exchanges are scrambling to tighten security on fears of a wave of potentially AI-assisted hacks, even as regional governments embrace tokenization and stablecoins.

India’s national cybersecurity agency warned that an AI-assisted threat actor has escalated its attacks on cryptocurrency exchanges and other financial businesses, stealing wallet keys and siphoning funds.

The Indian Computer Emergency Response Team (CERT-In) on Wednesday designated the threat actor as “CRAFTY FALCON” and explicitly identified crypto exchanges among affected businesses.

“The threat is active and ongoing. CSIRT-Fin continues to receive reports of successful attacks, including from NBFCs and fintech entities that had already received the earlier restricted advisories and had undertaken remediation,” said CERT-In.

The warning adds to evidence of hackers using AI tools against financial institutions. In October, cybersecurity firm CrowdStrike reported that an unidentified attacker used ARTEX, an AI penetration-testing tool also named in CERT-In’s advisory, alongside Claude Code and other AI models to breach South Korean financial firms and steal data.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission has urged domestic crypto exchanges to increase the proportion of assets held in cold wallets after the $6.3 billion attack on Binance.

The FSC said it held an emergency meeting with other regulators, cybersecurity agencies and crypto service providers to review security measures.

It reviewed exchanges’ IT access controls, wallet withdrawal approval and signing procedures, and administrator privileges. It also urged providers to increase the proportion of assets held in offline cold wallets.

But South Korean law already requires exchanges to hold at least 80% of users’ crypto assets in cold storage, with Upbit keeping 99.17% assets in cold wallets, followed by Bithumb at 94.76% and Coinone at 85%.

The Bank of Korea is surveying 31 financial institutions on how a major exchange shutdown, prolonged crypto downturn or losses at another firm could affect their operations.

One scenario asks how financial firms would be affected if South Korea’s largest crypto exchange by market share temporarily suspended operations following a major hack or similar incident.

Other questions address a steep fall in cryptocurrency prices followed by a sustained trading slump, and the potential fallout if another financial institution suffered heavy losses from a crypto-related incident.

Despite clear evidence of years of North Korea-linked hacking groups stealing billions from crypto exchanges, Pyongyang claims to have had no involvement in the $6.3 billion Binance hack and floated the idea it was actually the Americans.

“The FBI is trying to brand the DPRK as a ‘cyber criminal state’, claiming that it obtained information that a hacking organization ‘supported’ by the DPRK was involved in the incident,” said Kim Yo Jong said. “But the US assertion is an absurd rhetoric and nothing new.”

South Korea’s government has backed postponing tax on cryptocurrency gains by another two years to 2029. Judging on the troubled history of the tax, it would not be surprising if it is delayed again at that point.

Crypto.com will discontinue its consumer app services in Hong Kong on Feb. 1, 2027, while continuing to pursue regulatory approval for its institutional exchange.

A Crypto.com spokesperson told Cointelegraph that the company would discontinue its consumer app services in Hong Kong. The spokesperson said all affected customers had received advance notice and would receive instructions on closing their accounts and support to withdraw their funds.

Nearly three years after the local entity submitted an application for a license and the status remains “deemed-to-be-licensed” — which a transitional status rather than full regulatory approval.

The Hong Kong headquartered Animoca Brands has secured approval to manage investment funds for non-retail clients in Abu Dhabi.

The authorization, granted to subsidiary Animoca Asset Management (AD) Limited by Abu Dhabi Global Market’s Financial Services Regulatory Authority (FSRA), allows it to oversee funds that pool money from professional investors rather than the general public.

It expanding the asset management business of a company that began in mobile games before moving into blockchain gaming, rode the Metaverse wave and was a big player in venture investing.

Hong Kong has expanded its Project Ensemble pilot to allow round-the-clock settlement of tokenized transactions, as the city’s monetary regulator prepares to bring regulated stablecoins into trials involving tokenized government debt.

Hong Kong has recently officially introduced secondary market trading for SFC-authorised Tokenized Funds through licensed exchanges. Two China Asset Management money-market funds can now trade continuously via PantherTrade and the Futubull app.

Thailand’s securities regulator has filed a criminal complaint against BG Limited, the operator of cryptocurrency exchange Bitget, for allegedly operating without a license. Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission will block access to the website and app from December 5.

According to the SEC, Bitget offered crypto trading services between 2022 and 2025, charged trading fees and promoted its services in Thai through its websites and social media channels. The regulator said these activities violated Thailand’s digital asset business licensing requirements.

The regulator advised users to take action regarding assets held on the platform before the deadline.

Australia’s central bank raised its interest rate by 25 basis points to 4.85% on Tuesday, warning that inflation would stay elevated through much of 2027 as energy costs and AI-related investment added to price pressures.

The Reserve Bank of Australia’s monetary policy board voted 6–3 for the increase, with three members preferring to hold rates at 4.60%.

The bank pushed back its forecast for inflation to return to the top of its 2% to 3% target range until late 2027, around three quarters later than previously expected.

The Australian government’s decision to scrap the 50% capital gains discount for all investments in the May budget came as a big shock for crypto owners. The new scheme effectively doubles the taxable component of a gain, minus a small discount for inflation.

Some Aussie crypto holders are taking drastic measures — like Finder founder and crypto king Fred Schebesta who is relocating to Singapore which offers 0% CGT, stating the tax changes were a “killer blow.”

But there are less dramatic methods to reduce CGT bills including setting up a self managed super fund (SMSF), a company to trade crypto at the company tax rate, or to hope the opposition will reverse the changes after the next election.

Japanese yen stablecoin JPYC marked its first anniversary with a circulating supply around 3 billion yen—one quarter of its internal target.

BlackRock, BlackRock Japan, SBI Holdings and Startale have signed an MOU to explore connecting tokenized money-market funds with SBI’s JPYSC yen stablecoin.

Ministry of Finance rules require cryptocurrency exchanges licensed in Vietnam to report security incidents and remediation plan within 24 hours

Five major banks—including HSBC, JPMorgan’s Kinexys, MUFG, Société Générale-Forge under BNP Paribas, and Standard Chartered—have launched the Global Layer One Organization (GL1) in Singapore. The non-profit aims to promote standards for tokenized financial markets.

#5112. Tesla to Wind Down Cybertruck Production as Elon Musk Pivots to Robotaxis and AI

Tesla to Wind Down Cybertruck Production as Elon Musk Pivots to Robotaxis and AI

Tesla will stop making the Cybertruck at its Texas Gigafactory around the middle of next year, CEO Elon Musk said on Friday, as the company shifts its focus to autonomous vehicles.

"Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production," Musk tweeted.

Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production

Responding to a user who called the Cybertruck the best vehicle Tesla has ever made, Musk replied: "True, but the Cybertruck fleet will be around for a long time." "Cybertruck was an incredible technology development platform and lives on through Cybercab," Wes Morrill, the truck's lead engineer, wrote in response to Musk's post, thanking the truck's owners and "everyone who contributed to making the future looking like the future."

The stainless-steel pickup, first delivered to customers in late 2023, has struggled to win buyers. Tesla delivered 8,295 vehicles in its "other models" category, which includes the Cybertruck, in the third quarter, down 48% from a year earlier, Electrek reported. That puts the truck's sales at roughly a tenth of the 250,000 units a year that Tesla built its production line to make, according to the outlet.

Musk has made the Cybercab, Tesla's robotaxi, central to his push to turn the carmaker into an AI and robotics company. In April 2025, he said Tesla would launch its first robotaxi service in Austin using Model Y vehicles running its Full Self-Driving software, and begin building Cybercabs in Texas the following year.

Tesla began offering rides in the Cybercab, a two-seater with no steering wheel or pedals that was first unveiled in October 2024, in limited areas of Austin on September 3, Reuters reported. The National Highway Traffic Safety Administration said at the time that it was "evaluating the situation," as federal rules typically require cars to have human controls. Tesla began producing the Cybercab in April, and had 45 of them registered in Texas as of the launch, according to the report.

At the 2024 unveiling, Musk said he expects the Cybercab to cost around $30,000, Auto Express reported.

The shift comes as Musk weighs combining Tesla with SpaceX, which absorbed his AI company xAI earlier this year. Last month, Musk said both companies' boards had formed special committees of independent directors to evaluate a merger, which would reshape his AI empire by bringing rockets, satellites, AI models, robots and cars under one roof. A SpaceX filing has since confirmed that any combination would need sign-off from its "controlling shareholder," Musk.

A merger would also create a Bitcoin treasury behemoth, combining SpaceX's 18,712 BTC and Tesla's 11,509 BTC for a total of 30,221 BTC, worth roughly $2.3 billion at current prices.

Both companies have also blocked their shares from being offered on the tokenized stock venue being built by crypto exchange OKX and NYSE owner ICE.

#5110. Tesla appears to be preparing to sell US-built Model 3s in Canada again

Tesla Canada has updated the Model 3 Premium Rear-Wheel Drive on its website with 584 km of range and a 0-100 km/h time of 5.2 seconds. Those are the exact specs of the Fremont-built Long Range RWD that Tesla sold in Canada two years ago.

The Shanghai-built version, the $39,490 CAD car Canadians haven’t been able to order since early October, is rated at 463 km and 6.2 seconds. The new numbers point to a different car from a different factory.

Canadian Tesla owner RJ was first to spot the change on X:

Tesla Canada 🇨🇦 has updated Model 3 Premium RWD range and 0-100 specs.New specs:584 km range (previously 463 km)0-100 in 5.2 seconds (previously 6.2 seconds)Note: Premium Model 3 RWD orders are currently paused because the Government of Canada states Tesla has exhausted its… pic.twitter.com/PeWP15w2RS

The new figures are in the “Model 3 Premium Specs” section of Tesla’s Canadian Model 3 page, under the Rear-Wheel Drive tab, along with a curb weight of 1,745 kg. The page’s marketing copy now also promises “up to 584 km (EPA est.) on a single charge.”

Tesla hasn’t touched its Canadian comparison page, though. As of this afternoon, it still lists the Model 3 Premium RWD at 463 km, 6.2 seconds and $39,490.

In October 2024, Tesla Canada listed the Model 3 Long Range RWD at 584 km and 5.2 seconds, starting at $54,990 and eligible for the federal iZEV rebate. That car came from Fremont.

They also line up with the Model 3 Premium RWD Tesla sells in the US today. Tesla’s US website rates it at 363 miles of EPA range, which converts to 584 km, with 0-60 mph in 4.9 seconds. It starts at $42,490 USD.

There’s one other candidate. Giga Shanghai builds a Model 3 Long Range RWD for the Chinese market with a 78.4 kWh pack, 830 km of CLTC range and the same 5.2-second 0-100 km/h time.

But a Shanghai-built car runs into the same quota problem that paused Model 3 orders in Canada earlier this month. Tesla’s website says orders will resume “if Govt of Canada issues more quota, expected March 2027.” Meanwhile, the federal tracker showed 33,069 of the period’s permits still unused as of October 9.

So if Tesla wants to sell a long-range Model 3 in Canada before March, it has to come from Fremont.

And that’s where it gets expensive. Canada’s 25% counter-tariffs on US-made vehicles, in place since April 2025, still apply. The Department of Finance’s counter-tariff page, updated October 9, says the measures against US autos “continue to apply.”

Those tariffs are what pushed Tesla out of US-built Model 3s in the first place. When they hit, Tesla raised the Model 3 Long Range AWD by $11,000 to $79,990 and dropped the $55,990 RWD entirely. In March, it shipped its remaining US-built Model 3 inventory out of Canada ahead of the switch to Shanghai, which led to the record-low $39,490 Model 3 Premium RWD in May.

If Tesla applies a similar markup to a Fremont-built Premium RWD, it would land somewhere around $65,000 to $67,000. That’s well above the $50,000 transaction cap of Canada’s new $5,000 EV Affordability Program, even though US-built cars otherwise qualify through CUSMA (Chinese-built ones don’t).

Right now, the only Model 3 you can order in Canada is the Performance, which Tesla priced at $74,990 when it cut its price in May.

Do Canadians really want to buy from a guy helping the trade war against Canada? I hope not.

Tesla hasn’t announced anything, and the configurator hasn’t changed. But Tesla doesn’t usually rewrite a spec sheet for a car it isn’t planning to sell, and these numbers match a car it has sold in Canada before.

The most logical explanation is a stopgap. Tesla had a real hit with the $39,490 Model 3, ran through its quota in about four months, and is now stuck selling a $75,000 Performance until March. A Fremont-built Premium RWD would give buyers a long-range Model 3 at something less painful than that.

The problem is that it’d be a tough sell. I think Canadian buyers are more likely to wait and see if Tesla gets more quotas next and year. At the same time, they will wait to see what the Chinese automakers have to offer next year.

#5109. $3.1B in Stolen Binance Crypto Has Yet to Be Laundered: Elliptic

$3.1B in Stolen Binance Crypto Has Yet to Be Laundered: Elliptic

Around half of the $6.43 billion stolen from Binance last month has yet to be laundered, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said in a report updated on Friday.

About $3.11 billion remains in addresses linked to the theft, mostly in ETH and Bitcoin, and those funds are "very likely" still held by North Korea, Elliptic said. A further $2.85 billion, or 44% of the haul, has been frozen or seized, mostly stablecoins that issuers such as Tether and Circle froze within an hour of the theft. That compares with around 3% frozen after the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025, according to Elliptic.

Binance hack update: the XRP has started moving. About $50 million has been swapped, mostly through THORChain into BTC, with about $226 million still to move.

Bitcoin converted from other stolen assets is now about 1,650 BTC ($124 million), almost twice the figure a day earlier.…

The FBI has attributed the October 29 theft to North Korean hackers it calls TraderTraitor. North Korea has denied involvement.

The laundering so far has been "highly complex and clearly automated," Elliptic said. It has used more than 20,000 wallets on 16 blockchains, at least 36 services and at least 65,000 transactions, roughly one new wallet every 30 seconds.

The 18,270 BTC stolen from Binance, worth about $1.5 billion at the time of the theft, was split into 19 addresses on the day of the hack and has not moved since. The nine largest Ethereum addresses, holding 89% of the attacker's ETH, have not moved since October 30.

Most of the laundering so far, about $220 million in TRX, has run through Tron, where a single distribution wallet sent 684.6 million TRX to hundreds of new addresses over 51 hours, including through the night in Pyongyang. Almost all of it was bridged to Ethereum.

All of the stolen Solana, about $87 million at current prices, has been swapped, bridged or mixed, and the attacker has started laundering the stolen XRP, with about $50 million converted so far and $226 million remaining. In total, about $124 million has been converted into Bitcoin, much of it through cross-chain service THORChain.

The speed has fueled speculation that AI agents are moving the funds, but Elliptic said the evidence points to conventional automation. "The laundering is clearly automated, but nothing we have seen shows an AI agent making decisions," the firm said.

Elliptic expects the attacker to swap the ETH for Bitcoin and then cash out through OTC brokers and exchanges over a period of months. After the Bybit hack, North Korea laundered all 499,000 stolen ETH within 10 days but took months to cash out, the firm noted.

Binance has continued to restore services. On Friday, the exchange said users could now verify that their balances are backed at least 1:1 using the zero-knowledge proof for its latest proof-of-reserves snapshot, and that it aims to reopen withdrawals for all tokens on restored networks by the end of next week.

#5107. The 2027 Kia EV6 is over $8,000 more than the new EV3

Kia announced that 2027 EV6 prices start at about $38,000, or $8,000 more than the new EV3.

The 2027 Kia EV6 arrives as a carryover model from last year. After reducing prices on the 2026 model year by $5,000 just a few months ago, the 2027 EV6 remains the same price across all trims.

The base EV6 Light standard-range RWD model starts at $37,900, not including the $1,545 destination fee, which is the same as last year.

Powered by a 63 kWh battery, the standard-range variant offers up to 237 miles of range. Upgrading to the EV6 Light long range RWD model costs an extra $3,300, starting at $41,200, but delivers up to 319 miles of range using a larger 84 kWh battery.

The 84 kWh battery offers up to 319 miles of range in RWD variants, while AWD EV6 models get up to 295 miles. The top-spec EV6 GT-Line AWD has up to 270 miles of range.

Kia said that with the EV6 MSRP unchanged and the launch of the new EV3, it’s “making EVs more attainable for those who embrace the fully electric lifestyle.”

The 2027 Kia EV3 starts at just $29,890, or about $8,000 less than the EV6. Kia’s entry-level electric SUV is available in five trims: Light, Wind, Land, GT-Line, and GT.

The lowest-priced EV3 Light FWD model uses a 58.3 kWh battery and delivers an EPA-estimated range of up to 221 miles.

Higher-priced trims, including the Wind, Land, GT-Line, and GT, use a larger 81.4 kWh battery that delivers up to 321 miles of range on a single charge. Like the EV6, the EV3 is also available with front-wheel-drive and all-wheel-drive powertrain options.

Inside, both the EV6 and EV3 feature Kia’s ccNC infotainment system. The setup includes nearly 30″ of screen space in a curved panoramic display, including dual 12.3″ instrument cluster and central navigation screens, plus a 5″ climate control touchscreen.

Both also include a native NACS port so that you can access Tesla Superchargers without a separate adapter.

However, the EV6 uses an 800V architecture, enabling faster charging than the EV3, which uses a 400V system.

I've had four EVs and the 800v charging system on my 2025 Ioniq 5 is the biggest improvement I have experienced in any EV. My road trip stops are now about 20 minutes to go from 15% to 85% at fast chargers. To me, this is a game changer. When people talk about range anxiety, they are really talking about long recharging times and with these speeds, you'll be back on the road in the time it takes to hit the restroom and grab a bit to eat.

The EV6 can charge from 10% to 80% in about 18 minutes, whereas the EV3 takes about 30 minutes.

The EV6 is also larger and less boxy than the EV3, measuring 184.8″ long, 74″ wide, and 60.8″ tall, with a 114.2″ wheelbase. In comparison, the EV3 is 169.3″ long, 72.8″ wide, and 62″ tall, with a 105.5″ wheelbase.

#5106. Anthropic IPO Valuation Could Be Slashed in Half to $1T After AI Stock Rout: Report

Anthropic IPO Valuation Could Be Slashed in Half to $1T After AI Stock Rout: Report

Major investors are increasingly doubtful that Anthropic's initial public offering can go ahead at its expected valuation of $1.5 trillion, after a sharp sell-off in AI stocks, The Wall Street Journal reported on Thursday.

Anthropic, which filed last month to list on Nasdaq under the ticker "ANT," has been aiming to go public the week before Thanksgiving and raise up to $100 billion, according to the Journal. But three major investors who plan to take part in the IPO told the paper the valuation will likely need to come down, with one suggesting it could be closer to $1 trillion.

The company could also raise less than planned, perhaps around $80 billion, one investor told the Journal. Another wondered whether Anthropic might delay the offering until market conditions improve. Anthropic executives and advisers are expected to meet in the coming days to discuss the plans, according to the report.

The doubts follow a rout in AI stocks on Thursday, as investors weighed whether Democratic gains in the midterms could bring stricter oversight of data center construction. The S&P 500 closed down 4.4% and the tech-heavy Nasdaq 100 fell 6.3%, both their worst days since April 2025, while the Philadelphia semiconductor index slid 12.3% and Nvidia dropped 9.2%, NBC News reported. Stocks partly recovered on Friday morning: as of 11:21 a.m. ET, the Nasdaq Composite was up 1.4% and Nvidia had gained 3.4%, according to Yahoo Finance.

Democrats are planning bills to shield consumers from rising electricity costs tied to data centers, require independent testing of advanced AI systems and clarify when AI companies can be held liable for harm, Reuters reported, though any legislation would likely face steep odds while Trump remains in the White House. The party also plans closer scrutiny of leading AI companies, including Anthropic, according to the report.

Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-VT) has called for a "national data center moratorium," while Texas governor-elect Gina Hinojosa has pledged to pause data center approvals in a state that is home to more than a quarter of planned U.S. data center capacity, CNBC reported.

A delay to Anthropic's IPO is now an "escalating risk" in the current environment, technology analyst Richard Windsor told The Telegraph. "A postponement would protect against near-term valuation risks and would be more likely if the correction sustains over a longer period," said Nalin Patel, an analyst at PitchBook, according to the paper.

Some investors want to see Anthropic's October results before assuming a cut. The company's annualized revenue run rate topped $80 billion at the end of October, and it had $146.6 billion in cash as of September 30, according to the Journal.

Traders have pushed back their expectations. On Polymarket, the odds that Anthropic lists by November 20 stood at 43% on Friday, while the chance of a listing by the end of the year was 71%. On Kalshi, where traders bet on when Anthropic will officially confirm its IPO, the odds of that happening before December 1 fell 15 points to 59%, while the chance of it happening before January 1 slipped to 68%. On Decrypt Predict, a prediction market developed by Decrypt's parent company, Dastan, traders also put the odds of an Anthropic IPO before 2027 at 71%.

The valuation now in question is already well below earlier expectations. In September, Decrypt reported that a leaked draft filing showed Anthropic was seeking a valuation of around $2 trillion.

#5105. American Bitcoin’s $126M profit masks mining pressure as rivals turn to AI

The Eric Trump-backed miner reported $126 million in quarterly net income, largely driven by gains on its Bitcoin holdings, despite rising mining costs and shrinking margins.

American Bitcoin reported a quarterly net income of $126 million on Wednesday, but rising mining costs and falling profits from mining raise questions about its long-term strategy as major rivals pivot towards artificial intelligence.

The Eric Trump-backed company’s profit was driven by a $197.3 million noncash gain on its Bitcoin (BTC) holdings. Meanwhile, mining revenue fell 5.5% to $63.4 million from the previous quarter, while the cost to mine one BTC rose about 15% to $42,100.

Excluding gains from its Bitcoin holdings, American Bitcoin would have reported an operating loss of approximately $5.5 million, according to its financial statements. The company also recorded a separate $64.3 million loss on derivatives.

“Despite seasonal power cost pressures, we continued mining Bitcoin at a structural discount to spot,” American Bitcoin CEO Mike Ho said in Wednesday’s earnings announcement.

Earnings per share stood at $1.73, beating analysts’ consensus estimate of $0.26, according to Yahoo Finance data.

Bitcoin was trading at $76,119, down 5% over the past 24 hours as of 8:50 p.m. UTC, according to CoinGecko data. Shares of American Bitcoin (ABTC), meanwhile, closed Thursday down about 15% at $5.31.

The results highlight an interesting divide among Bitcoin miners: while American Bitcoin continues mining and holding BTC, competitors like Riot Platforms, MARA Holdings and Core Scientific are investing in AI to find new sources of revenue.

David Gamble, founder of asset management company Enigma Holdings, which focuses on Bitcoin mining and AI infrastructure, said American Bitcoin’s reported profit does not tell the full story of its mining business.

“Ultimately, mining Bitcoin below its market price doesn’t automatically mean you’re creating shareholder value,” Gamble told Cointelegraph.

American Bitcoin’s gross mining profit fell to $25.8 million from $33 million in the previous quarter. Its reported mining margin dropped to 41% from 49%, leaving the company with a smaller share of mining revenue after direct costs.

The company’s $63.4 million in quarterly revenue also fell short of analysts’ estimates of $74.35 million, according to Yahoo Finance data, a gap of nearly 15%.

The company mined 891 BTC during the quarter, down from 932 BTC in the previous three months. It also said higher electricity prices were the main reason mining costs increased.

“I don’t necessarily think their accumulation strategy is flawed, but I do think it raises questions about long-term sustainability,” Gamble said, pointing to increasing mining difficulty and the need to keep investing in newer and more efficient technology to compete.

Compared with the same period last year, revenue was nearly unchanged, falling from $64.2 million to $63.4 million. Reported profit, however, jumped from $3.5 million to $126 million, largely reflecting gains on the company’s Bitcoin holdings.

Related: Bitcoin miners face widening AI financing costs as lenders scrutinize deals

American Bitcoin’s strategy comes as competitors like Riot Platforms, MARA Holdings and Core Scientific rapidly expand into AI.

Riot is building large AI data centers, according to its latest earnings. Meanwhile MARA is expanding into AI while continuing to mine Bitcoin. Core Scientific, on the other hand, has already shifted much of its business toward AI data centers.

American Bitcoin, by contrast, continues building its BTC reserves, holding 8,427 BTC at the end of September, up from 3,418 BTC a year earlier.

“The real competitive advantage in this industry is shifting toward energy infrastructure,” Gamble said. “It’s no longer just about how much Bitcoin you can mine or how much hashrate you can deploy.”

Gamble also explained that Bitcoin miners and AI companies are competing for the same power supply, and AI data centers could make more money than Bitcoin mining in some areas.

“That doesn’t mean every mining facility can simply transition into AI,” he said. “You still have to account for depreciation, equipment replacement, financing costs, and the opportunity cost of deploying that capital elsewhere.”

Magazine: Bitcoin turns 18: Has the original rebel grown up?

#5103. 65 Chicago Interior Designers to Know From the AD PRO Directory

Based in America’s third-largest city, these Chicago interior designers and architects have widely varied styles: from bold and bursting with color to chic, minimal, and restrained. These talents from the AD PRO Directory are at the top of their game, attracting the Windy City’s most discerning clients and tackling everything from glamorous Gold Coast penthouses to modern farmhouse-style family retreats in Hinsdale. Whether you’re contemplating a new build, a full renovation, or just a refresh, scroll on to discover 65 studios to hire for your next project in Chicago and beyond, and visit their Directory profiles for more images, information, and contact details to get in touch.

In a Lincoln Park home, Abrams Valenti Interiors set the tone in a bedroom by placing a screen from the client’s previous home behind a Dmitriy & Co. Brampton bed.

Precision and artistry go hand in hand in the designs of Abrams Valenti Interiors. Founded by principal Michael Abrams nearly 25 years ago, the firm offers residential design and interior architecture services for clients across the United States, from a contemporary Miami home to a mountain retreat in Utah. Interiors are imbued with comfort and sophistication and are intentionally designed to enhance the lives of those who occupy them. Abrams and his team pride themselves on personalized client service, and each project has a dedicated creative team that works with Abrams to bring the home to life.

Aishah Nisar designs homes that are meant to be lived in and enjoyed. The founder and creative director of Aishah Nisar Interior Design focuses on creating a balance of practicality and style in her spaces, adding unique details to showcase the clients’ personal style. Nisar and her team work hard to understand their clients’ unique needs and to guide them through the whole process. #5102. Lectric launches XP Lite3 e-bike, the new best low-cost model in the US

Lectric eBikes, the largest electric bike company in the US by just about any metric you can measure, has just unveiled its latest model: the Lectric XP Lite3. And with a starting price of just $799, the new lightweight folding electric bike is continuing the company’s tradition of packing surprisingly impressive features into remarkably affordable e-bikes.

The XP Lite2 had been widely considered the best bang-for-your-buck option in the ultra-budget category, offering an affordable yet punchy Class 2 e-bike for a song, despite still coming from a reputable US-based company with strong reputation for warranty and support.

Now, the third-generation model builds on what has become one of the most successful budget e-bike platforms in the country, bringing a long list of upgrades designed to make the bike easier to ride, fold, transport, and live with.

And perhaps most importantly, Lectric has managed to keep that familiar $799 starting price that debuted on the original XP Lite1 all the way back in 2022.

The new Lectric XP Lite3 weighs just 49 lb (22.2 kg), making it one of the lighter options in Lectric’s extensive lineup of folding electric bikes. That’s still no featherweight, partly due to Lectric’s insistence on not neutering the bike’s performance, but it’s still a solid 10-20 lb lighter than the average direct-to-consumer e-bike these days.

The bike retains the compact folding design that made its predecessors popular with everyone from college students to RV owners, but introduces a redesigned frame with improved ergonomics and a reinforced folding latch borrowed from the company’s flagship XP4 model.

New quick-release pedals further reduce the bike’s storage footprint, making it easier to squeeze into a car trunk, apartment closet, or RV storage compartment.

Despite its compact dimensions, the XP Lite3 can support a maximum payload of 275 lb (125 kg) and accommodates riders ranging from 4’8” to 6’2”.

The bike reaches speeds of up to 20 mph (32 km/h) and offers up to 40 miles (64 km) or 80 miles (128 km) of range in Eco mode, though expect significantly less range when using higher-power pedal assist or throttle-only riding. The range discrepancy comes from two different battery offerings. The base model features a 374 Wh battery, while the long-range version has nearly twice the capacity at 672 Wh.

Power comes from Lectric’s Stealth M24 motor, which is rated at 300W continuous but over 800 peak watts. The motor combines with Lectric’s pedal assist selector to offer five levels of pedal assist that are personalizable, letting riders dial in exactly how much boost they’d like on their ride. Riders also get a twist throttle, a more comfortable style of throttle enabled by the lack of shifters crowding the bars on this single-speed e-bike.

Several other upgrades help the XP Lite3 punch above its weight class, including integrated rear turn signals, a bright 3.5-inch full-color display brought over from other recently launched models in the Lectric lineup, and a redesigned LevelUp Lite rear rack with new mounting possibilities.

Stopping power comes from Star Union 602 hydraulic disc brakes with thicker 2.3 mm rotors, another welcome feature on a bike at this price point.

Lectric is also offering a Gates Carbon Belt Drive version of the XP Lite3, giving riders a cleaner, quieter, and virtually maintenance-free alternative to a traditional chain drivetrain. That Gates belt drive model fetches a $100 premium, but is likely worth it for those who value the cleaner, maintenance-free alternative that is likely to outlast the bike itself.

The chain-drive version comes in Arctic White, Blue Mist, Matte Grey, and Matte Lavender, while the belt-drive model is offered in JW Black and Ballerina Pink.

It’s difficult to overstate just how much Lectric has changed the American electric bicycle industry.

The Phoenix, Arizona-based company has grown into the country’s largest e-bike brand by both sales volume and the total number of e-bikes on the road. In doing so, it has become one of the most recognizable names in affordable electric transportation.

But Lectric didn’t get there by simply building cheap electric bikes.

Instead, the company helped revolutionize what consumers should expect from a budget-friendly e-bike, combining capable hardware, practical designs, and aggressive pricing with something that has historically been missing from much of the low-cost e-bike market: a real American company standing behind the product.

While countless inexpensive e-bikes sold online come from little-known overseas brands with questionable long-term support, Lectric has built its reputation around US-based customer service, accessible replacement parts, and a company that riders can actually reach when something goes wrong.

That’s a big part of what has made the XP Lite series so important.

Since its introduction, the XP Lite series has arguably set the standard for what an entry-level electric bike should be. It isn’t necessarily the most powerful, fastest, or fanciest e-bike on the road, but it delivers the essentials at a price that opens electric cycling to an entirely new audience.

And importantly, it does so without requiring riders to gamble on an unknown brand.

The XP Lite3 continues that formula, adding features like hydraulic disc brakes, integrated turn signals, and a color display that would have been difficult to imagine on a $799 e-bike just a few years ago.

I’ve been following Lectric since the company’s earliest days, and it’s been fascinating to watch how the brand has consistently raised the bar for affordable electric bikes. I rode one of Lectric’s first 10 bikes off their nascent production line all the way back in 2019, and I think they’d agree that few people outside the company know the brand better than I do. So I can say that, without a doubt, this is one of the company’s most important launches to date because of how it revolutionizes how much bike someone can get without stretching their budget farther than they have to. And at a time when inflation is still rising unchecked and fuel prices are through the roof, this is what the industry needs more than ever.

Keep in mind that the $799 price tag for the XP Lite3 announced today is the same price that the original XP Lite carried back in 2022, despite that value ballooning to $939 in 2026 dollars due to rising inflation.

What impresses me most isn’t necessarily any single feature on the new XP Lite3. It’s the combination of all those features at a price that remains accessible to so many riders.

Hydraulic brakes, a color display, integrated turn signals, a refined folding mechanism, and even the option for a Gates belt drive? That’s quite a lot of bike for the money. And remember, the performance is still there. This is still a 48V e-bike with a legit 20 mph (32 km/h) top speed on throttle and pedal assist.

Sure, there are lighter e-bikes out there, and there are certainly more premium models with fancier components. But that’s not really the point of the XP Lite.

The point is to make electric biking accessible without making it feel like a compromise at every turn.

And when you consider that you’re getting all of this from the biggest e-bike company in the country, complete with US-based support and a proven track record, it’s hard to argue with the value proposition.

The original XP Lite helped establish what a good affordable electric bike could be. The XP Lite3 looks ready to move that benchmark forward once again.

And on a personal note, not only do I want my own Lectric XP Lite3 now, but I think my wife’s new e-bike will soon be an XP Lite3 in Ballerina Pink with a Gates belt drive. Remember, “Happy wife, happy life!”

Micah Toll is a personal electric vehicle enthusiast, battery nerd, and author of the Amazon #1 bestselling books DIY Lithium Batteries, DIY Solar Power, The Ultimate DIY Ebike Guide and The Electric Bike Manifesto.

The e-bikes that make up Micah’s current daily drivers are the $999 Lectric XP 2.0, the $1,095 Ride1Up Roadster V2, the $1,199 Rad Power Bikes RadMission, and the $3,299 Priority Current. But it’s a pretty evolving list these days.

#5101. Core Scientific to quit Bitcoin mining by year-end as AI business surges

The former Bitcoin mining heavyweight plans to become a pure-play data center provider in 2027, with mining now accounting for just 7.5% of quarterly revenue.

Core Scientific said it expects to exit Bitcoin mining by the end of 2026 as it shifts its business toward artificial intelligence and high-performance computing infrastructure.

“We expect to complete the wind-down during the fourth quarter and enter 2027 as a pure-play, high-density colocation provider,” said Core Scientific chief financial officer Jim Nygaard during a Q3 earnings call on Thursday.

Core Scientific had previously disclosed in a July quarterly filing that hosted mining would end by Dec. 31 as it continued to wind down its self-mining operations. The comment establishes a targeted completion date for its complete Bitcoin mining exit.

Its latest quarterly results show how far the business has shifted. Third-quarter self-mining revenue fell to $12.3 million from $57.4 million a year earlier, while revenue from hosting other miners dropped to $5.2 million from $8.7 million.

Bitcoin mining generated just $17.5 million, or 7.5%, of Core Scientific’s $232.9 million in third-quarter revenue, compared with $215.4 million from colocation services.

The company had nearly 50% fewer miners online at the end of September than at the end of 2025, according to Nygaard.

Core Scientific’s move into AI hosting took shape in 2024 after signing an initial hosting agreement with CoreWeave in March that year, followed by a much larger deal announced in June.

At the time, Core Scientific presented AI hosting as an expansion alongside its mining business. Its June 2024 announcement emphasized preserving Bitcoin mining capacity.

By December 2025, The Wall Street Journal reported that Core Scientific was already targeting to wind down its Bitcoin mining operations by 2028.

Related: American Bitcoin’s $126M profit masks mining pressure as rivals turn to AI

Mining economics added pressure in the months following.

Core Scientific recorded $266.5 million in mining-related asset impairments in the first quarter of 2026, citing weaker Bitcoin prices, historically low hashprice and falling digital asset mining equipment values in the secondary market.

During the second quarter, it terminated its mining equipment agreement with Block and Proto, recording a $41.9 million loss as part of the transition.

The planned exit comes as the US election results introduce fresh uncertainty for companies expanding AI infrastructure.

Opponents of data-center development were among the winners of the Democrats’ sweeping midterm gains, Reuters reported Wednesday.

The backlash centers partly on electricity bills and the demands large facilities place on local resources.

Cointelegraph reached out to Core Scientific for comment.

#5099. Crypto Liquidations Top $1 Billion as Bitcoin Tests 11-Week Low

Crypto Liquidations Top $1 Billion as Bitcoin Tests 11-Week Low

Bitcoin is trading near $75,570 on Friday after touching $74,780.91 today; its lowest intraday reading since August 21, 77 days earlier.

The crypto market retreat followed a brutal selloff in AI stocks and another round of forced closures in derivatives markets.

According to CoinGlass data, $1.05 billion in crypto have been liquidated over the preceding 24 hours. Long positions, which profit when prices rise, accounted for $930.04 million, or 88.2%, compared with $124.69 million in shorts.

That 80%-20% gap in liquidations also matches the overall sentiment tracked by the Fear and Greed Index which is set at 28 points (approaching extreme fear) out of 100, where 0 is a state of maximum fear among traders, and 100 is maximum greed. For context, the index was at a neutral just a week ago, before the midterm elections.

Bitcoin accounted for $267.20 million of the total, with 88.9% from long positions, while Ethereum recorded $167.14 million, 81.1% of it long.

Liquidation happens when an exchange automatically closes a position because its collateral is no longer sufficient, sometimes adding selling pressure to an existing decline. Unlike normal trading, in which investors can handle any unrealized loss, trading with leverage gives a maneuver window that can end in the loss of all funds once an asset goes beyond or below a specific price.

The post-midterm election fear is spreading on Wall Street, and U.S. technology stocks took the first big hit on Thursday. The Nasdaq Composite fell 5.52%, its worst session in 19 months, while the Philadelphia semiconductor index plunged more than 12% and Nvidia alone lost over 9%.

Democratic victories in Tuesday's midterm elections raised concerns that tougher AI regulation could slow construction of the data centers behind this year's chip rally. The S&P 500 lost 4.36% on Thursday, erasing roughly $3 trillion in market value across its constituent companies.

Bitcoin had already given back its post-election relief rally as chipmakers sank, as Decrypt reported Thursday. Forced closures of bullish crypto positions accelerated the fall, though simultaneous moves in stocks and tokens doesn’t necessarily mean the equity rout alone caused Bitcoin's decline.

A stronger-than-expected U.S. October jobs report also increased expectations of another Federal Reserve rate hike in December. Higher borrowing costs can weigh on speculative markets, leaving crypto exposed even as chip shares recover.

Right now, Bitcoin trades near $75,570, with the current candle still forming after a low of $74,780.91. The weekly chart shows another red candle and a sequence of lower highs since September, when Bitcoin briefly traded above $87,000.

The 50-day exponential moving average, or EMA, gives more weight to recent prices and sits near $79,756, above the 200-day EMA around $76,497. That ordering remains bullish on a longer view, but Bitcoin is now trading below both averages, a sign the latest decline has overtaken the lagging bullish signal.

The Relative Strength Index, or RSI, reads 37.7, below the neutral 50 mark but still above the 30 level generally considered oversold. The Average Directional Index, or ADX, is at 19.7, typical of trendless states—but the negative directional indicator exceeds the positive one, pointing to weak but bearish directional pressure.

The immediate support is $74,780, the horizontal line and intraday low on the supplied chart. A daily close below it would shift attention toward the $73,000 to $72,500 area around August's breakout, followed by the psychological $70,000 level if that zone fails.

On a recovery, Bitcoin first faces the 200-day EMA near $76,497, then some weak and immediate near $80,000. Friday's daily candle is still open, with its $74,780.91 low the immediate level to defend.

#5098. Ford’s new sub-$30K electric truck can power your home for a lot less

Ford wants to make powering your home with an EV a lot cheaper. Its upcoming sub-$30,000 Fathom electric truck will feature a new bidirectional charging system that ditches the need for an expensive external inverter.

Arriving in 2027, the Fathom will be the first EV from a US automaker priced below $30,000 that can deliver more than 10 kW of AC power to a home without a separate wall-mounted inverter.

And Ford says it’s cutting the cost of the other equipment needed for home backup power, too.

EV batteries store electricity as direct current (DC), but our homes use alternating current (AC). To send power from an EV battery into a house, traditionally, that meant installing a pricey external inverter.

Ford has come up with a different approach for its new Universal EV Platform, which will underpin the Fathom.

After acquiring startup Auto Motive Power in 2023, Ford redesigned its bidirectional charging system from the ground up. Instead of relying on a separate wall-mounted inverter, it built one directly into the truck’s E-box, one of the vehicle’s five main modules.

Fewer parts mean lower costs, and Ford says the savings will extend to the home charging equipment, too.

Fathom owners will have two ways to use the truck’s battery to power their homes.

Home Power Management will let owners charge their truck when electricity rates are low, then use that stored power at home when rates are higher. That could mean real savings for households on time-of-use electricity plans. They’ll need Ford’s new Home Power Station, which the company expects to cost about half as much as its first-generation bidirectional charger.

Home Backup Power will let the truck keep the lights on during an outage. For that, owners will need both the Home Power Station and Ford’s Home Backup Panel. Ford says the backup hardware should cost at least 60% less than its first-generation equipment.

Ford hasn’t released prices for either piece of hardware, so we don’t yet know what a complete home installation will cost.

But pairing a sub-$30,000 electric truck with substantially cheaper home power equipment could make bidirectional charging a lot more appealing.

Making the hardware cheaper is one thing. Making sure utilities can handle thousands of EVs that send electricity back into homes and potentially the grid is another.

Ford formed a working group in July with American Electric Power, BC Hydro, Consumers Energy, Exelon, and Pacific Gas and Electric. They’re working on making it easier for customers to connect bidirectional EVs before the Fathom arrives next year.

For example, someone using their truck solely for home backup power during an outage shouldn’t have to go through the same approval process as someone sending electricity to the grid.

The group is also looking at how to update utility procedures to accommodate newer bidirectional charging standards and make it easier for EV owners to participate in virtual power plants (VPPs). In other words, the batteries in parked EVs could become a useful source of electricity when the grid needs it most.

Ford says the Fathom’s system is designed to meet applicable UL 1741 requirements and IEEE 1547 interconnection standards. It also uses SAE J3068 communications technology, which Ford says helps reduce charger costs.

The idea is to get the technical requirements and utility processes sorted out before affordable bidirectional EVs start arriving in much larger numbers.

Affordable EVs already offer some form of bidirectional power. Two of the cheapest electric cars in the US, the Chevy Bolt and Nissan Leaf, are both available for under $30,000.

The Bolt supports vehicle-to-home (V2H) power through GM Energy equipment, while the Leaf offers vehicle-to-load (V2L) capability through an external device plugged into its charging port.

Ford’s system takes things much further. It can deliver more than 10 kW of AC power to a home without an expensive external inverter, and Ford says the rest of the equipment will cost considerably less, too.

That’s a pretty compelling proposition, especially for people who live in areas prone to power outages or have electricity plans with higher peak-hour rates.

When power outages last a while the area still affected shrinks. While a whole county may be out day one, by day 3 it may just be your neighborhood left. At that point you could drive across town and recharge the car and be good to power your house for another few days.

There are still some unknowns. Ford hasn’t announced the final hardware prices, and installation costs will vary. Plus, utility rules need to catch up if EV owners are going to take full advantage of bidirectional charging.

But this seems to be going in the right direction. If automakers can bring costs down and the industry can agree on consistent standards, having an EV that doubles as a home battery could eventually become a normal part of EV ownership rather than an expensive bonus feature.

It has hundreds of pre-vetted solar installers competing for your business, ensuring you get high-quality solutions and save 20-30% compared to going it alone. Plus, it’s free to use, and you won’t get sales calls until you select an installer and share your phone number with them.

#5097. This 322-Square-Foot Apartment Gives Classic Style a Contemporary Update

Some gifts are best kept a surprise until the moment they’re handed over to their recipient. That was the case with this small 322-square-foot apartment located in the Vidny Gorod residential complex, about four miles from Moscow, which the owner, Lara, received as a gift from her daughter, Marta, and her son-in-law. “The couple personally oversaw the renovation, keeping the project a secret until the very end,” says interior designer Polina Chumakova, founder of Lagolga Buro, who oversaw the design. “Lara knew nothing about it and didn’t see the space that they were going to give her until it was completed and she first set foot in her new home.”

In the living room, the custom cabinet by Avantiz complements a vintage table and chairs by Tok. On the tabletop, an arrangement of vintage vases by Mak.buro adds a decorative touch.

Lara’s passion for cooking and baking guided some of the design choices, as her daughter envisioned turning the 322-square-foot apartment into a home capable of welcoming the entire family. “It was important to create a comfortable space not only for cooking but also for spending time together, so that on weekends the apartment could be filled with the aroma of homemade desserts,” explains Chumakova. “Lara has two children—Marta and her brother—and two grandchildren, ages 13 and 9. The family often gets together on weekends and at the holidays.”

The apartment’s contemporary-meets-classic design reflects Vidny Gorod’s architecture and its urban context. “The style was carefully defined, choosing solutions suited to both the home’s size and the budget for this project,” says Chumakova. “We invested in high-quality finishes and carefully selected furniture, lighting, and accessories. The idea was to combine a contemporary, understated design with natural walnut accents and a few vintage details. The result is an interior with a classic character, yet without references to any specific era.”

The wall of the living space is painted an olive green by Little Greene that adds depth and warmth. The Synergy Grace sofa bed by Ormatec is paired with the Firmo bedside cabinet by AM.PM; a vintage wall sconce on the left and an Umbrella floor lamp by Artefact on the right complete the scene. On the wall hangs a work by Ksusha Dmitrieva.

There were certainly some challenges. The original floor plan had issues that required a careful reorganization of the space. “The builder had left a rather bulky load-bearing pillar, as well as two separate small bathrooms. It wasn’t possible to move the column, so we decided to integrate it into the layout, concealing it inside a large closet in the entryway,” explains Chumakova. “This allowed us to create a more functional space while making the structure less noticeable. We also combined the two bathrooms. This created a more comfortable home and also provided additional storage.”

The kitchen was custom-designed by Avantiz and includes elegant vintage pendants.

Another challenge was the lack of natural light. “The apartment receives little direct sunlight, so we paid close attention to the choice of colors and finishes,” says Chumakova. “For example, the light, milk-colored porcelain tile, initially selected for the floor, appeared much more beige in the space than we had expected. We replaced it and also adjusted the other colors to account for this effect, striving to keep the interior as bright and warm as possible.” The lighting design also took into consideration the various needs of daily life, without relying on single light sources. “We planned several different lighting scenarios: integrated ceiling lighting, pendant lamps, accent lights for display cases, and other spotlights,” explains Chumakova. “Throughout the apartment, there are about five different types of lighting.”

To make the best use of space, the design focuses on versatile furniture and solutions that serve multiple purposes, helping to avoid clutter in the rooms. “Instead of the classic light fixture above the dining table, we chose a solution that would maintain the open feeling of the space and not limit the table to a specific position,” says Chumakova. “We also opted for a sofa bed instead of a traditional bed. This allowed us to keep more open space and make the room more versatile: During the day, it can be used as a place to relax and spend time with the family.”

In the kitchen, as in the entryway, the floor is covered with white tiles with black square accents, a graphic detail that picks up the colors of the vintage lighting fixtures.

The layout, however, prioritizes the kitchen, designed to be the heart of this home. “We deliberately wanted to dedicate a significant portion of the apartment to it, because it’s one of the most important areas of the home for its owner,” says Chumakova. “The work area is located near the window overlooking a courtyard: It’s a pleasant spot to spend time cooking or simply looking at the scene outside.”

All the storage systems were custom-designed to blend seamlessly with the interiors. “We wanted the cabinets to be almost invisible,” says Chumakova. “There are two large storage units in the apartment: one in the entryway and one in the living room.”

In the living room, custom cabinets by Avantiz are embellished with retro details. The Dovlet House rug, the vintage table, and the chairs combine contemporary pieces with retro charm.

The color choices help define the different areas without visually fragmenting the space. “We used color to distinguish the entryway from the living room, while the ceiling and the doors of the built-in closet in the entryway were painted the same shade,” Chumakova explains. A natural color palette and materials with distinct textures were chosen to create a warm and inviting atmosphere. “The main colors are olive green in the entryway and a milky white in the sofa bed area,” says Chumakova. “More intense touches of orange appear only in certain details, such as the pouf’s upholstery and the floor lamp. Everything complements the natural walnut, one of the key materials in the interior.”

The entryway features walls painted in an olive green from Little Greene paint and a white tile floor dotted with small black accents. A vintage pendant, and the orange Justyna pouf from La Redoute that adds a vibrant pop of color, complete the look.

In the entryway, the floor is covered with white tiles dotted with small black accents, in keeping with the contemporary classic style. The moldings, found in the entryway and on the wall behind the sofa, add rhythm and depth to the surfaces, giving a modern twist to a traditional detail.

The small size of the apartment required some very careful choices when it came to furnishings. “It was important to find furniture on the right scale: pieces that wouldn’t overwhelm the small apartment and that, at the same time, would be consistent with the contemporary classic aesthetic,” says the interior designer. “So we reinterpreted some traditional elements, from walnut moldings to vintage lamps with distinctive shapes, adapting them to a modern residential complex and the compact dimensions of the apartment.”

In the bath, the wooden cabinet is custom-made by Avantiz; the Evana fixtures by Villeroy & Boch and the Oxford faucet by Migliore complement the vintage sconces.

The finished apartment won over Marta, who oversaw every aspect of the renovation along with her husband. “It was a perfectly successful experiment,” the designer says. “Perhaps, if it had been a more conventional project with the future owner, it would have involved them in every phase and it would have been more challenging. It’s possible that some of the bolder solutions might not have been approved.” In this case, the fact that Marta wanted to surprise her mother also provided greater design freedom, resulting in a space that would be perfectly suited to its future resident.

This article originally appeared in AD Italia.

#5095. Crypto Card Provider Gnosis Pay Says Hackers Accessed Customer Data

Crypto Card Provider Gnosis Pay Says Hackers Accessed Customer Data

Crypto card provider Gnosis Pay said Friday that hackers accessed customer information through the system behind its own branded cards, the latest in a string of security incidents across the industry.

The company’s monitoring systems detected unauthorized access on Nov. 3, and containment began within two hours, Gnosis Pay said in a blog post. It has since replaced the compromised security credentials and fixed the vulnerability. Customer funds weren’t affected, the company said.

The breach was limited to Gnosis Pay’s consumer business, whose platform was already scheduled for retirement on Dec. 20. Its enterprise platform, which powers white-label card programs for fintech firms, wallets and neobanks, wasn’t affected.

Gnosis Pay didn’t say how many customers were affected or what data was exposed, but said it emailed each affected customer with details of what was taken from their account.

“While funds were not affected, the security of personal information is just as important and we take full responsibility,” the company wrote on X.

Gnosis Pay said it has stepped up monitoring of card activity and wallets for fraud, and has made 3D Secure, an extra verification step, mandatory for all online card payments until the consumer platform is retired. It has reported the breach to its partners, its card issuer and data protection authorities, and is arranging specialist monitoring for signs that the data is misused.

We had a data breach affecting the system powering Gnosis Pay-branded cards (our B2C business line) that resulted in the exposure of certain customer information.

Everyone affected was sent an email today with full information.

We deeply regret that this happened. While funds…

— Gnosis Pay 💳 (@gnosispay) November 6, 2026

The company urged customers to be wary of unexpected messages about their cards, even ones that include real personal details or purchases. It said it will never ask for seed phrases, PINs or full card numbers, or ask users to move funds.

It also told customers to be careful about sharing their address or details of their crypto holdings, both online and offline.

That last warning reflects a growing threat. Physical “wrench attacks” on crypto holders, including home invasions and kidnappings, were on pace for a record year after more than $30 million was stolen in the first half of 2026, according to blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis.

The disclosure follows several other recent examples of breaches in crypto security. Beyond the record-setting $6.4 billion hack of the world’s largest crypto exchange in Binance, rival trading platform Coinbase said last week that a breach at analytics vendor Amplitude exposed some customer contact data.

Last month, hardware wallet maker Ledger said all confirmed cases in a suspected $87 million drain involved devices bought from CryptoBilis, a Southeast Asian reseller, and urged recent buyers to move their funds to new devices.

#5094. BYD names its ultra-luxury EV, and it could be a game changer next week

BYD’s new flagship EV will be its largest and most luxurious electric sedan yet, potentially debuting two firsts for the company next week: Rolls-Royce-style coach doors and solid-state batteries.

BYD is better known for its low-cost electric cars, like the Seagull, a little hatchback that sells for under $10,000 in China.

The Chinese auto giant has greatly expanded its portfolio over the past few months and years. BYD now offers everything from entry-level EVs to three-row flagship SUVs, pickup trucks, electric supercars, and just about everything in between.

BYD has three sub-brands: Denza, FangChengBao, and Yangwang. It also has two main families under the BYD brand: the Dynasty and Ocean series.

Yangwang sits at the top of BYD’s brand hierarchy and showcases its latest breakthrough technologies.

Previously, Yangwang vehicles debuted on BYD’s quad-motor e4 platform, delivering a whopping 1,300 horsepower and featuring its DiSus-X intelligent body control system, which enables the vehicle to “dance,” jump, and drive on three wheels.

For its next model, BYD is pushing further into the elite segment to take on ultra-luxury brands like Rolls-Royce, Bentley, and Mercedes-Maybach.

BYD revealed the name of its flagship ultra-luxury EV for the first time on Tuesday: the Yangwang Altior, along with a new teaser of the partially uncovered executive sedan.

Coming soon#YANGWANG #YANGWANGALTIOR pic.twitter.com/kFnu6X6csq

Yangwang general manager Hu Xiaoqing said the name was chosen as an independent Chinese-English naming scheme. In China, it goes by DingYao. The “Ding” symbolizes stability and endurance, while “Yao” signifies radiant brilliance, or shining brightly like a star in the sky.

The English name Altior comes from Latin for “higher,” representing Yangwang’s relentless pursuit of ever-greater heights.

The new teaser comes after Hu revealed several images of the new Yangwang executive sedan testing for the first time last month.

Although it was disguised, it appeared to be about the same shape and size as the Mercedes-Benz Maybach S-Class or Rolls-Royce Phantom. Local reports indicate it will be around 5,500 mm (216″) long.

HU confirmed for the first time last week that the global launch of the new flagship sedan will take place in the UAE, not in BYD’s home market of China.

BYD has not yet revealed a specific launch date, but the Yangwang sedan could make its first appearance as early as next week. According to CarNewsChina, citing “sources familiar with the project,” BYD is aiming to reveal the new flagship model in Dubai, UAE, around November 5.

Zheng Yu, Yangwang’s executive general manager, said the new model will feature “unprecedented disruptive technology,” but didn’t reveal specifics.

Although details are still limited, the ultra-luxury sedan was shown at BYD’s Flash Charging station, suggesting it will support megawatt flash charging.

It also features rear-hinged coach doors, a first for BYD. The flagship EV will reportedly be the first to feature BYD’s solid-state batteries.

BYD executive vice president Stella Li said during an interview with Carwow.es last month that the company’s first vehicle to use solid-state batteries will debut in 2027.

Li said BYD is “in the leading position” in solid-state battery technology and its commercialization, and the company plans to prove it next year.

BYD’s battery unit, FinDreams, plans to begin production of sulfide-based all-solid-state batteries in 2027. It will initially be produced in limited batches, then scale to mass production closer to the end of the decade.

The part about solid state batteries got my attention. I'm looking forward to learning more about how they perform!

While BYD has yet to confirm it, introducing the new battery tech in a flagship model would make sense.

The executive sedan is expected to debut around November 5. Prices are rumored to start in the 2 million yuan range, or about $300,000.

Yangwang’s current electric sedan, the U7, starts at 658,000 yuan ($98,000). The brand also sells the U8 (and U8L), a plug-in hybrid luxury off-road SUV, and the U9, a fully electric super-coupe.

The extended U8L is Yangwang’s highest-priced mass-produced vehicle, starting at 1.45 million yuan ($217,000).

#5093. Crypto exchanges tout cold storage ratios after Binance attack

Coinbase says it keeps more than 99% of customer assets offline, while Gate reports 88.26% of its total assets are kept in cold storage.

Crypto exchanges were quick to reassure users following the attack on Binance’s hot wallet infrastructure last week, with several companies disclosing how they divide assets between cold storage and internet-connected hot wallets this week.

Crypto exchange Gate on Thursday launched a “Proof of Security” (PoSEC) framework, reporting that 88.26% of its total assets were in cold storage and 11.74% in hot wallets. It claims its cold storage could cover 104.58% of customer assets even if its hot wallets were completely drained.

“By publicly disclosing the distribution of assets between platform cold storage and hot wallets, PoSEC provides visibility into asset storage structures and their ability to cover potential losses under extreme scenarios,” Gate said. It noted the data is still undergoing independent review by a third-party audit firm.

Threat actors attacked Binance’s hot-wallet infrastructure last week, stealing approximately $6.36 billion, most of it in about 20 minutes, according to TRM Labs. Binance CEO Richard Teng said the “vast majority” of user assets are held in cold wallets, but the company has not provided a precise split.

Other crypto exchanges have been quick to share their cold storage ratios this week.

In a blog post Wednesday, Coinbase said that more than 99% of customer assets are held in offline cold storage and that it continuously rebalances excess assets to cold storage.

“Binance’s reported hot wallet incident has understandably left customers asking how Coinbase protects customer assets from something similar happening here,” it said.

On Tuesday, Upbit, the largest cryptocurrency exchange in South Korea, confirmed it holds 99.17% of its assets in cold wallets, with the remaining 0.83% in hot wallets as of September 2026. These figures are already disclosed to the Financial Supervisory Service.

“This represents the highest cold wallet storage ratio among South Korea’s five major KRW-based cryptocurrency exchanges and is substantially above the regulatory minimum of 80%,” a spokesperson for Upbit parent company Dunamu told Cointelegraph, in response to questions about South Korea’s call for crypto exchanges to increase their cold storage ratios.

“Our aim is to keep our hot wallet holdings at an absolute minimum, with a target of maintaining them below 1% of customer digital assets,” they added.

Related: Fed, OCC check US banks after Binance hack as experts flag security risks

Other major South Korean exchanges have reported lower cold-wallet shares. Financial Supervisory Service figures cited in earlier reporting put Bithumb at 94.76%, Coinone at 85%, Digital X, formerly Korbit, at 82.79%, and GOPAX at 80.54% at the end of September.

OKX’s existing security overview, last updated on Sept. 18, 2026, said it holds more than 95% of client crypto assets in cold wallets and less than 5% in hot wallets.

The reported ratios are not necessarily directly comparable because exchanges use different reporting periods and may calculate cold-storage shares against different asset bases.

Cold storage ratios, however, offer only part of the picture of an exchange’s security.

Offline wallets can still be exposed through compromised signing systems, inadequate access controls or employees approving fraudulent transactions.

Hong Kong’s Securities and Futures Commission warned in an August 2025 circular that such weaknesses could compromise both hot- and cold-wallet infrastructure.

Magazine: Asian crypto exchanges batten down the hatches as hacks surge: Asia Express

#5090. US Sanctions 45 More Targets Tied to Alleged Crypto Scam Empire Prince Group

US Sanctions 45 More Targets Tied to Alleged Crypto Scam Empire Prince Group

The U.S. Treasury Department has sanctioned four individuals and 41 entities that it says support Prince Group, the Cambodian conglomerate accused of running scam compounds that use trafficked workers to defraud victims through crypto investment and romance scams.

The new designations, announced on Thursday by the Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC), target the offshore companies, nominee owners and administrators that Treasury says have helped Prince Group executives conceal assets and evade earlier sanctions imposed in October 2025 and June 2026.

"Cyber scam syndicates continue to steal billions of dollars from American victims while relying on sophisticated international business networks to launder their illicit proceeds," Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said.

Among those sanctioned is Qiu Yong, who Treasury said controls a network of British Virgin Islands and Hong Kong companies on behalf of Prince Group, including money lenders and wine businesses, and who has worked for a company controlled by Prince Group chairman Chen Zhi. His personal assistant, Zhao Mengyao, was also designated.

OFAC also targeted two people it said are holding property for, or running companies with, Prince Group executive Chen Bo, who was sanctioned in June. They include his wife, He Yujing, to whom Chen Bo transferred ownership of his Tokyo mansion after the October 2025 sanctions, according to Treasury, and associate Visal Pang, who controls 11 Cambodian companies spanning construction, logistics, a hotel and a "super app." Eleven companies still controlled by Chen Bo were also sanctioned, including Prince International Airlines and CB General Insurance.

Further entities in Singapore, Mauritius and Malaysia were sanctioned for links to Prince Group associate Dai An, along with a Singapore media company owned by alleged Prince Group finance chief Thet Li.

Separately, OFAC designated Chui Kok Wa, a Malaysian national based in Madagascar, who Treasury said led a network of scammers that used crypto and other technologies to defraud Americans of more than $400,000.

The U.S. is seeking the forfeiture of about $14 billion in Bitcoin tied to Prince Group, while UK authorities have frozen the London penthouse of Thet Li, Decrypt reported last month. Prince Group has denied the allegations.

Chen Zhi himself was arrested in Cambodia in January along with two other Chinese nationals and extradited to China, where state media described him as the head of a major cross-border gambling and fraud syndicate, the BBC reported. Cambodia, which had revoked his citizenship the month before, also placed the group's Prince Bank into liquidation.

Thursday's action was coordinated with the FBI, the Justice Department's Scam Center Strike Force, which has reported $580 million in crypto seizures and freezes, and the U.S. Attorney's Office for the District of Alaska, with help from authorities in the British Virgin Islands and the UK, according to Treasury.

Interpol declared scam compound networks a global threat last year, citing their use of human trafficking, online fraud and coerced labor. In September, the Treasury's Financial Crimes Enforcement Network linked about $12.7 billion in reported financial activity to suspected crypto investment scams, largely run by criminal organizations operating scam compounds in Southeast Asia.

The UN Office on Drugs and Crime estimated in July that scam operations across East and Southeast Asia, Australia and New Zealand cost victims between $88.3 billion and $114.1 billion in 2025, much of it through crypto investment fraud run from compounds holding people from at least 80 countries.
